\dot{{\bf u}}=(\dot{u}_{1},\ldots,\dot{u}_{n})\in{\bf u}T^{n}
, set

C_{\dot{{\bf u}}}=C_{\dot{u}_{1}}\times C_{\dot{u}_{2}}\times\cdots\times C_{\dot{u}_{n}}.

(4.22)

One then has the isomorphisms

\displaystyle C_{\dot{{\bf u}}}\times B\longrightarrow B{\bf u}B,\;\;(c_{1},\,c_{2},\,\ldots,\,c_{n},\,b)\longmapsto[c_{1},\cdots c_{n-1},c_{n}b]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}},

(4.23)

\displaystyle C_{\dot{{\bf u}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}=B{\bf u}B/B,\;\;(c_{1},c_{2},\ldots,c_{n})\longmapsto[c_{1},c_{2},\ldots,c_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}},

(4.24)

\displaystyle B\times C_{\dot{{\bf u}}}\longrightarrow B{\bf u}^{-1}B,\;\;(b,c_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,c_{n-1}^{\prime},c_{n}^{\prime})\longmapsto[b(c_{n}^{\prime})^{-1},\,(c_{n-1}^{\prime})^{-1},\,\ldots,\,(c_{1}^{\prime})^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}}.

(4.25)

For
c=(c_{1},c_{2},\ldots,c_{n})\in C_{\dot{{\bf u}}}
, set
[c]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}}=[c_{1},\ldots,c_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}}
and
\underline{c}=c_{1}c_{2}\cdots c_{n}\in G
.
Define

{\mathcal{G}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}=\{(c,b,b_{-},c^{\prime}):c,c^{\prime}\in C_{\dot{{\bf u}}},\,b\in B,\,b_{-}\in B_{-},\,\underline{c}\,b=b_{-}\underline{c^{\prime}}\}\subset C_{\dot{{\bf u}}}\times B\times B_{-}\times C_{\dot{{\bf u}}}.

(4.26)

We then have the isomorphism
{{\mathcal{I}}}_{\dot{{\bf u}}}:{\mathcal{G}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}\to\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}
given by

{{\mathcal{I}}}_{\dot{{\bf u}}}((c_{1},\ldots,c_{n}),\,b,\,b_{-},(c_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,c_{n}^{\prime}))=[c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1},c_{n}b,\,(c_{n}^{\prime})^{-1},(c_{n-1}^{\prime})^{-1},\ldots,(c_{1}^{\prime})^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{2n}}.

Proposition 4.8
.

For any
{\bf u}\in W^{n}
and
\dot{{\bf u}}\in{\bf u}T^{n}
,
{{\mathcal{I}}}_{\dot{{\bf u}}}
is an isomorphism of Lie groupoids over
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}
,
where the groupoid
\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}\rightrightarrows{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}
is given as in Theorem
4.4
, and

{\mathcal{G}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}\rightrightarrows{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}
is the groupoid defined in
[
24
]
as follows:

\displaystyle\mbox{source map}\;\theta_{+}:\;{\mathcal{G}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u},\;\;(c,\,b,\,b_{-},\,c^{\prime})\longmapsto[c]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}},

\displaystyle\mbox{target map}\;\theta_{-}:\;{\mathcal{G}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u},\;\;(c,\,b,\,b_{-},\,c^{\prime})\longmapsto[c^{\prime}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}},

\displaystyle\mbox{unit map}\;\epsilon:\;{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}},\;\;[c]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}}\longmapsto(c,\,e,\,e,\,c),

\displaystyle\mbox{inverse map}\;{\rm inv}:\;{\mathcal{G}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}},\;\;(c,\,b,\,b_{-},\,c^{\prime})\longmapsto(c^{\prime},\,b^{-1},\,b_{-}^{-1},\,c),

\displaystyle\mbox{multiplication}:(c,\,b,\,b_{-},\,c^{\prime})(c^{\prime},\,b^{\prime},\,b_{-}^{\prime},\,c^{\prime\prime})=(c,\,bb^{\prime},\,b_{-}b_{-}^{\prime},\,c^{\prime\prime}).

Proof.

By (
4.23
) and (
4.25
),

{{\mathcal{I}}}_{\dot{{\bf u}}}
is an isomorphism of varieties. The fact that
{{\mathcal{I}}}_{\dot{{\bf u}}}
is an isomorphism from
the groupoid
{\mathcal{G}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}\rightrightarrows{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}
to
\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}\rightrightarrows{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}
follows directly from the
definitions of the two groupoids.

Q.E.D.

Remark 4.9
.

Under the isomorphism
C_{\dot{{\bf u}}}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u},c\mapsto[c]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}}
, one can regard
{\mathcal{G}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}\rightrightarrows{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}
as a
groupoid
{\mathcal{G}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}\rightrightarrows C_{\dot{{\bf u}}}
, and as a such, it is a sub-groupoid of
the direct product

C_{\dot{{\bf u}}}\times B\times B_{-}\times C_{\dot{{\bf u}}}\rightrightarrows C_{\dot{{\bf u}}}

of two groupoids: the pair groupoid

C_{\dot{{\bf u}}}\times C_{\dot{{\bf u}}}\rightrightarrows C_{\dot{{\bf u}}}
and the direct product group
B\times B_{-}

as a groupoid over the one point space, where we identify

C_{\dot{{\bf u}}}\times B\times B_{-}\times C_{\dot{{\bf u}}}\longrightarrow C_{\dot{{\bf u}}}\times C_{\dot{{\bf u}}}\times B\times B_{-},\;\;(c,\,b,\,b_{-},\,c^{\prime})\longmapsto(c,\,c^{\prime},\,b,\,b_{-}).

4.3.
Generalized double Bruhat cells as Poisson groupoids

We now give the third isomorphic model of the special
configuration Poisson groupoids using
generalized double Bruhat cells
.
For
n\geq 1
, recall the right action of
G^{n}
on itself given in (
1.1
).
Let

\widetilde{F}_{-n}=G\times_{B_{-}}\cdots\times_{B_{-}}G,

the quotient of

G^{n}
by
B_{-}^{n-1}\times\{e\}\subset G^{n}
, and let
\widetilde{\pi}_{-n}
be the Poisson structure on
\widetilde{F}_{-n}

that is the (well-defined) projection of the Poisson structure
(\pi_{\rm st})^{n}
on
G^{n}
. For

(g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n})\in G^{n}
, denote its image in
\widetilde{F}_{-n}
as

[g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{-n}}
. For any integers
m,n\geq 1
, we introduce in
\lx@sectionsign
C.1
a Poisson
structure
\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n}
on
\widetilde{F}_{m}\times\widetilde{F}_{-n}
, which is the sum of the product Poisson structure

(\widetilde{\pi}_{m},\widetilde{\pi}_{-n})
with a certain
mixed term
. See Definition
C.1
. The Poisson structure
\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n}
is
T
-invariant
under the
T
-action on
\widetilde{F}_{m}\times\widetilde{F}_{-n}

given by

t\cdot([g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{m}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m}},[k_{1},k_{2},\ldots,k_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{-n}})=([tg_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{m}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m}},[tk_{1},k_{2},\ldots,k_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{-n}}).

(4.27)

Introduce the sub-manifold

G_{m,n}=\{([g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{m}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m}},[k_{1},k_{2},\ldots,k_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{-n}}):g_{1}g_{2}\cdots g_{n}=k_{1}k_{2}\cdots k_{n}\}\subset\widetilde{F}_{m}\times\widetilde{F}_{-n}.

(4.28)

For
{\bf u}=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{m})\in W^{m}
and
{\bf v}=(v_{1},\ldots,v_{n})\in W^{n}
, let again
B{\bf u}B
be the image of

(Bu_{1}B)\times\cdots\times(Bu_{m}B)
in
\widetilde{F}_{m}
, let

B_{-}{\bf v}B_{-}\subset\widetilde{F}_{-n}
be
the image of
(B_{-}v_{1}B_{-})\times\cdots\times(B_{-}v_{n}B_{-})
in
\widetilde{F}_{-n}
, and set

G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}=G_{m,n}\cap(B{\bf u}B\times B_{-}{\bf v}B_{-})\subset G_{m,n}.

(4.29)

It follows from the Bruhat decomposition of
G
that one has the disjoint union

G_{m,n}=\bigsqcup_{({\bf u},{\bf v})\in W^{m}\times W^{n}}G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}.

(4.30)

We prove in Corollary
C.4
that
G_{m,n}
is a
T
-invariant Poisson sub-manifold of
(\widetilde{F}_{m}\times\widetilde{F}_{-n},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})
, and that
(
4.30
) is the decomposition of
(G_{m,n},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})
into its
T
-leaves. Generalizing the case of

(G_{1,1},\widetilde{\pi}_{1,1})\cong(G,\pi_{\rm st}),

we call
G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}
, for any
{\bf u}\in W^{m}
and
{\bf v}\in W^{n}
, a
generalized double Bruhat cell
.
When
m=n
, the Poisson manifold
(\widetilde{F}_{n}\times\widetilde{F}_{-n},\widetilde{\pi}_{n,n})
, as well as the generalized double Bruhat cells
G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}
for
{\bf u},{\bf v}\in W^{n}
,
were introduced in
[
18
,
\lx@sectionsign
1.4]
.
We also note that for any
m,n
, the projections
(\widetilde{F}_{m}\times\widetilde{F}_{-n},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})\to(\widetilde{F}_{m},\widetilde{\pi}_{m})
and

(\widetilde{F}_{m}\times\widetilde{F}_{-n},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})\to(\widetilde{F}_{-n},\widetilde{\pi}_{-n})
to the factors are
Poisson. It follows in particular that for any
m\geq 1
, the map

(G_{m,1},\;\widetilde{\pi}_{m,1})\longrightarrow(\widetilde{F}_{m},\widetilde{\pi}_{m}),\;([g_{1},g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m}},g_{1}g_{2}\cdots g_{m})\longmapsto[g_{1},g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m}},

(4.31)

is a Poisson isomorphism.

Let now
{\bf u}=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n})\in W^{n}
, and consider the generalized double Bruhat cell
G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}}
. Fix any

\dot{{\bf u}}=(\dot{u}_{1},\ldots,\dot{u}_{n})\in{\bf u}T^{n}
. As
B_{-}uB_{-}=B_{-}C_{\dot{u}}
is a direct product decomposition
for any
u\in W
and any
\dot{u}\in uT
, one has
the isomorphism

B_{-}\times C_{\dot{{\bf u}}}\longrightarrow B_{-}{\bf u}B_{-},\;\;(b_{-},\,c_{1}^{\prime},\,c_{2}^{\prime},\,\ldots,\,c_{n}^{\prime})\longmapsto[b_{-}c_{1}^{\prime},\,c_{2}^{\prime},\,\ldots,\,c_{n}^{\prime}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{-n}}.

(4.32)

Recalling the definition of
{\mathcal{G}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}\subset C_{\dot{{\bf u}}}\times B\times B_{-}\times C_{\dot{{\bf u}}}
,
one then has the isomorphism

{\mathcal{J}}_{\dot{{\bf u}}}:\;{\mathcal{G}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}\longrightarrow G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}},\;(c,b,b_{-},c^{\prime})\longmapsto([c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1},c_{n}b]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}},[b_{-}c_{1}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime},\ldots,c_{n}^{\prime}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{-n}}),

(4.33)

where
c=(c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1},c_{n})
,
c^{\prime}=(c_{1}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime},\ldots,c_{n}^{\prime})
, and
(c,b,b_{-},c^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{G}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}
.

Definition 4.10
.

[
24
]
For each choice
\dot{{\bf u}}\in{\bf u}T^{n}
, define
the groupoid
G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}}\rightrightarrows{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}
such that

{\mathcal{J}}_{\dot{{\bf u}}}:\;{\mathcal{G}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}\longrightarrow G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}}

is a groupoid isomorphism
from the groupoid
{\mathcal{G}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}\rightrightarrows{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}
in Proposition
4.8

to
G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}}\rightrightarrows{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}
.

Using the parametrization
{\mathcal{J}}_{\dot{{\bf u}}}:{\mathcal{G}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}\to G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}}
, we can now define

\displaystyle E_{\dot{{\bf u}}}:\;G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}}\longrightarrow\Gamma_{2n},\;\;

\displaystyle([c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1},c_{n}b]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}},[b_{-}c_{1}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime},\ldots,c_{n}^{\prime}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{-n}})

(4.34)

\displaystyle\longmapsto[c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1},c_{n}b,\;(c_{n}^{\prime})^{-1},\ldots,(c_{2}^{\prime})^{-1},(c_{1}^{\prime})^{-1}]_{\widetilde{F}_{2n}},

where
((c_{1},\ldots,c_{n}),b,b_{-},(c_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,c_{n}^{\prime}))\in{\mathcal{G}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}
.

Theorem 4.11
.

For each
\dot{{\bf u}}\in{\bf u}T^{n}
,
the map
E_{\dot{{\bf u}}}
is a
T
-equivariant Poisson embedding of
(G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}},\widetilde{\pi}_{n,n})
into
(\Gamma_{2n},\widetilde{\pi}_{2n})

and gives a Poisson isomorphism

E_{\dot{{\bf u}}}:\;\;(G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}},\;\widetilde{\pi}_{n,n})\longrightarrow(\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})},\;\widetilde{\pi}_{2n})\subset(\Gamma_{2n},\widetilde{\pi}_{2n}).

Consequently, with the groupoid structure defined as in Definition
4.10
,

(G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}},\widetilde{\pi}_{n,n})\rightrightarrows({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u},\pi_{n})
is a Poisson groupoid,
and
E_{\dot{{\bf u}}}
is an isomorphism from the Poisson groupoid
(G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}},\widetilde{\pi}_{n,n})\rightrightarrows({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u},\pi_{n})
to the
Poisson
groupoid

(\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})},\widetilde{\pi}_{2n})\rightrightarrows({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u},\pi_{n})
.

Proof.

The fact that
E_{\dot{{\bf u}}}
is a Poisson isomorphism onto
(\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})},\widetilde{\pi}_{2n})
is a special case of Corollary
C.7

by taking
{\bf u}={\bf v}
and
E_{\dot{{\bf u}}}=E_{{\bf u},\dot{{\bf u}}}
. That
E_{\dot{{\bf u}}}
is a groupoid isomorphism
follows from the definition of the groupoid
G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}}\rightrightarrows{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}
and Proposition
4.8
.

Q.E.D.

Remark 4.12
.

We emphasize that the
groupoid structure on
G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}}
over
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}
depends on the choice of the representative
\dot{{\bf u}}\in{\bf u}T^{n}
. If
\hat{{\bf u}}=(\hat{u}_{1},\ldots,\hat{u}_{n})\in{\bf u}T^{n}
is another
such choice, and if
t\in T
is such that
\hat{u}_{1}\cdots\hat{u}_{n}=t\dot{u}_{1}\cdots\dot{u}_{n}
, then

E_{\dot{{\bf u}}}=E_{\hat{{\bf u}}}\circ r_{t}
, where
r_{t}:G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}}\to G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}}
is given by

r_{t}([g_{1},\ldots,g_{n-1},g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m}},[k_{1},\ldots,k_{n-1},k_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{-n}})=([g_{1},\ldots,g_{n-1},g_{n}t]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m}},[k_{1},\ldots,k_{n-1},k_{n}t]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{-n}}),

Thus
r_{t}:G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}}\to G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}}
defines a groupoid isomorphism from the groupoid structure on
G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}}
defined by
\dot{{\bf u}}
to that defined by

\hat{{\bf u}}
.

\diamond

5.
Configuration symplectic groupoids of flags

In this section, we assume that
G
is connected and simply connected.
Symplectic leaves of
({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T,\pi_{n}\bowtie 0)
, for any
n\geq 1
and
{\bf w}\in W^{n}
, are determined in

\lx@sectionsign
D
, where we also give a complete description of
the symplectic leaves of all the three series

(F_{n}^{o}\times T,\,\pi_{n}\bowtie 0),\hskip 14.45377pt(G_{m+n},\,\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n}),\hskip 14.45377pt(\widetilde{F}_{n},\,\widetilde{\pi}_{n}),\hskip 14.45377ptm,n\geq 1,

of
T
-Poisson varieties. See
\lx@sectionsign
D
for details.
For any
n\geq 1
and
{\bf u}\in W^{n}
, we show in this section that all the units of
the groupoid
\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}\rightrightarrows{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}
are contained in a single symplectic leaf, denoted as

\Lambda^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}
, of

(\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})},\widetilde{\pi}_{2n})
, and that
\Lambda^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}

is a Lie sub-groupoid
of
(\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})},\widetilde{\pi}_{2n})\rightrightarrows({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u},\pi_{n})
, obtaining thus a symplectic groupoid

(\Lambda^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})},\widetilde{\pi}_{2n})\rightrightarrows({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u},\pi_{n}).

Using Theorem
4.11
, we then show that every symplectic
leaf of
(G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}},\widetilde{\pi}_{n,n})
is symplectic groupoid over
({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u},\pi_{n})
.

5.1.
The symplectic groupoid
(\Lambda^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})},\widetilde{\pi}_{2n})\rightrightarrows({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u},\pi_{n})

Assume that
G
is connected and simply connected. Let
X^{*}(T)
be the character lattice of
T
. For
\lambda\in X^{*}(T)
and

t\in T
, write
t^{\lambda}
for the value of
\lambda
at
t
. Let
\Phi_{0}\subset X^{*}(T)
be the set of simple roots determined by the choice of
B
, and let

\{\omega_{\alpha}:\alpha\in\Phi_{0}\}\subset X^{*}(T)
be the corresponding set of fundamental weights.
For
u\in W
and
t\in T
, we also set

t^{u}=\dot{u}^{-1}t\dot{u}
using any
\dot{u}\in uT
.

Let now
{\bf u}=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n})\in W^{n}
be arbitrary. Define

{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf u}}}=\{t\in T:t^{\omega_{\alpha}}=1,\,\forall\,\alpha\in{\rm supp}^{o}({\bf u})\},

where
{\rm supp}^{o}({\bf u})=\{\alpha\in\Phi_{0}:u_{i}\omega_{\alpha}=\omega_{\alpha},\,\forall\,i\in[1,n]\}
.
Choose any
\dot{{\bf u}}=(\dot{u}_{1},\ldots,\dot{u}_{n})\in{\bf u}T^{n}
, and let again

C_{\dot{{\bf u}}}=C_{\dot{u}_{1}}\times\cdots\times C_{\dot{u}_{n}}
. Let
u=u_{1}u_{2}\cdots u_{n}\in W
.
Using the isomorphism
{{\mathcal{I}}}_{\dot{{\bf u}}}:{\mathcal{G}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}\to\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}
to write an element in

\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}
uniquely as

\gamma=[c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1},c_{n}b,\,(c_{n}^{\prime})^{-1},(c_{n-1}^{\prime})^{-1},\ldots,(c_{1}^{\prime})^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{2n}},

(5.1)

where
(c_{1},\ldots,c_{n}),(c_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,c_{n}^{\prime})\in C_{\dot{{\bf u}}}
,
b\in B,b_{-}\in B_{-}
, and
c_{1}\cdots c_{n-1}c_{n}\,b=b_{-}c_{1}^{\prime}c_{2}^{\prime}\cdots c_{n}^{\prime}
,
we define the sub-variety
\Lambda^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}
of
\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}
by

\Lambda^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}=\{\gamma\in\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}\,\mbox{as in \eqref{eq:gamma-1}}:\;[b]_{0}[b_{-}]_{0}^{u}=e,\,[b]_{0}\in\widetilde{T}^{{\bf u}}\}.

Note that
\Lambda^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}
contains all the units of the groupoid
\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}\rightrightarrows{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}
, and that

{\mathcal{I}}_{\dot{{\bf u}}}^{-1}(\Lambda^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})})={\mathcal{S}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}\{(c,b,b_{-},c^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{G}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}:\,[b]_{0}[b_{-}]_{0}^{u}=e,\,[b]_{0}\in\widetilde{T}^{{\bf u}}\}.

(5.2)

Note that
{\mathcal{S}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}
is a wide sub-groupoid of
{\mathcal{G}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}\rightrightarrows{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}
.
It thus follows from Proposition
4.8
that
\Lambda^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}
is a wide sub-groupoid of
\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}\rightrightarrows{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}
.

Theorem 5.1
.

For any
{\bf u}\in W^{n}
,
\Lambda^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}
is a symplectic leaf of
(\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})},\widetilde{\pi}_{2n})

and is a Lie sub-groupoid of
\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}\rightrightarrows{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}
.
Consequently,
(\Lambda^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})},\widetilde{\pi}_{2n})\rightrightarrows({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u},\pi_{n})
is a symplectic groupoid.

Proof.

Symplectic leaves of
(\Gamma^{\bf w},\widetilde{\pi}_{n})
for any
n\geq 1
and any
{\bf w}\in W^{n}

are described in Theorem
D.16
, and the case of
{\bf w}=({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})\in W^{2n}
is given in Example
D.18
.
More concretely, for
\gamma\in\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}
in (
5.1
), let

(c_{n+1},\ldots,c_{2n})\in C_{\dot{u}_{n}^{-1}}\times\cdots\times C_{\dot{u}_{1}^{-1}}
and
b_{n},\ldots,b_{1}\in B
be such that

b(c_{n}^{\prime})^{-1}=c_{n+1}b_{n},\;\;b_{n}(c_{n-1}^{\prime})^{-1}=c_{n+2}b_{n-1},\;\ldots,\;\;b_{2}(c_{1}^{\prime})^{-1}=c_{2n}b_{1}.

Then
\gamma=[c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1},c_{n},\,c_{n+1},\ldots,c_{2n-1},c_{2n}b_{1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{2n}}
and

[b_{1}]_{0}=[b]_{0}^{u^{-1}}
. Let

\dot{{\bf w}}=(\dot{u}_{1},\ldots,\dot{u}_{n},\dot{u}_{n}^{-1},\ldots,\dot{u}_{1}^{-1})\in{\bf w}T^{2n}.

We then
have the alternative description of
\Lambda^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}\subset\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}
as consisting of all

\gamma=[c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1},c_{n},\,c_{n+1},\ldots,c_{2n-1},c_{2n}b_{1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{2n}}\in\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})},

where
(c_{1},\ldots,c_{n},c_{n+1},\ldots,c_{2n})\in C_{\dot{{\bf w}}}
,
b_{1}\in B
, and
b_{-}=c_{1},\cdots c_{n}c_{n+1}\cdots c_{2n}b_{1}\in B_{-}

are such that

[b_{-}]_{0}\in{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf u}}}
and
[b_{1}]_{0}[b_{-}]_{0}=e
.
By Example
D.18
,
\Lambda^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}
is the symplectic leaf of
(\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})},\widetilde{\pi}_{2n})
passing through the point

[{\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{2n}}\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}\,[\dot{u}_{1},\ldots,\dot{u}_{n},\,\dot{u}_{n}^{-1},\ldots,\dot{u}_{1}^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{2n}}\in\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}.

We already know that

\Lambda^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}
is a sub-groupoid of
\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}\rightrightarrows{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}
. To show
that
\Lambda^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}
is a Lie sub-groupoid of
\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}\rightrightarrows{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}
, it remains to show that
the source map
\theta_{+}:\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}
restricts to a surjective submersion
\theta:=\theta_{+}|_{\Lambda^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}}:\Lambda^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}
. To this end, let

\Sigma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}=J_{2n}(\Lambda^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})})\subset{\mathcal{O}}^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}_{e}\times T.

Then

\Sigma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}
is the symplectic leaf of
\pi_{2n}\bowtie 0
in
{\mathcal{O}}^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}_{e}\times T
through the point

([\dot{u}_{1},\ldots,\dot{u}_{n},\,\dot{u}_{n}^{-1},\ldots,\dot{u}_{1}^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{2n}},\;e)\in{\mathcal{O}}^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}_{e}\times T.

By Example
D.15
,
\Sigma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}=\Sigma^{\dot{{\bf w}}}
, consisting of all

([c_{1},\ldots,c_{2n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{2n}},t)\in{\mathcal{O}}^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}_{e}\times T
, where
t\in{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf u}}}
and

(c_{1},\ldots,c_{2n})\in C_{\dot{{\bf w}}}
such that

c_{1}c_{2}\cdots c_{2n}\in B_{-}B\hskip 14.45377pt\mbox{and}\hskip 14.45377ptt^{2}=[c_{1},c_{2},\ldots,c_{2n}]_{0}.

We also know from Example
D.15
that the projection

P:\Sigma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}_{e},(q,t)\mapsto q,

is a
2^{|{\rm supp}({\bf u})|}
-to-
1
covering map, where

{\rm supp}({\bf u})=\Phi_{0}\backslash{\rm supp}^{o}({\bf u})
.
Let

p:\;{\mathcal{O}}^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}_{e}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u},\;[g_{1},\ldots,g_{n},g_{n+1},\ldots,g_{2n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{2n}}\longmapsto[g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}}.

Since
p
is a submersion, one sees that

\theta=p\circ P\circ J_{2n}:\Lambda^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}
is a submersion.

Q.E.D.

5.2.
Symplectic leaves in
G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}}
as symplectic groupoids

Let
{\bf u}\in W^{n}
and consider the Poisson manifold
(G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}},\widetilde{\pi}_{n,n})
. As
(G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}},\widetilde{\pi}_{n,n})
is a single
T
-leaf,
every symplectic leaf of
(G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}},\widetilde{\pi}_{n,n})
passes through the point
(\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}})\in G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}}
for some

\dot{{\bf u}}\in{\bf u}T^{n}
. Let
\dot{{\bf u}}\in{\bf u}T^{n}
, and let
S^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}
be the symplectic leaf
of
(G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}},\widetilde{\pi}_{n,n})
through
(\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}})
.
Using the parametrization
{\mathcal{J}}_{\dot{{\bf u}}}:{\mathcal{G}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}\rightarrow G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}}
in (
4.33
), we write every
{\bf g}\in G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}}
uniquely as

{\bf g}=([c_{1},\ldots,c_{n-1},c_{n}b]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}},[b_{-}c_{1}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime},\ldots,c_{n}^{\prime}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{-n}}).

(5.3)

where
((c_{1},\ldots,c_{n}),b,b_{-},(c_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,c_{n}^{\prime}))\in{\mathcal{G}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}
. Recall from (
5.2
) the wide sub-groupoid

{\mathcal{S}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}\rightrightarrows{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}
of the groupoid
{\mathcal{G}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}\rightrightarrows{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}
.
Recall also from Theorem
4.11
the Poisson isomorphism
E_{\dot{{\bf u}}}:(G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}},\widetilde{\pi}_{n,n})\to(\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})},\widetilde{\pi}_{2n})
.
The following Theorem
5.2
is now a direct consequence of Theorem
4.11
and
Theorem
5.1
.

Theorem 5.2
.

The symplectic leaf
S^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}
of
(G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}},\widetilde{\pi}_{n,n})
through
(\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}})
is given by

\displaystyle S^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}=E_{\dot{{\bf u}}}^{-1}(\Lambda^{{\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1}})=\{{\bf g}\in G^{{\bf u},{\bf u}}\,\mbox{as in \eqref{eq:bfg-0}}:\;[b]_{0}[b_{-}]_{0}^{u}=e,\,[b]_{0}\in\widetilde{T}^{{\bf u}}\}.

Equip
S^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}
with the structure of a Lie groupoid over
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u}
via the isomorphism

{\mathcal{J}}_{\dot{{\bf u}}}:{\mathcal{S}}^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}\to S^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}}
. Then

(S^{\dot{{\bf u}},\dot{{\bf u}}},\widetilde{\pi}_{n,n})\rightrightarrows({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf u},\pi_{n})
is a symplectic groupoid.

Appendix A
Mixed product Poisson structures

In
\lx@sectionsign
A.1
, we recall from
[
17
]
the construction of mixed product Poisson structures
using Poisson Lie group actions. We then show in
\lx@sectionsign
A.2
that the Poisson varieties

({\mathcal{C}}_{n},\widehat{\pi}_{n})
and
({\mathcal{B}}^{n},\overline{\pi}_{n})
defined in
\lx@sectionsign
3.3
are all mixed product Poisson varieties.

A.1.
Mixed product Poisson structures

The following definition was introduced in
[
17
]
.

Definition A.1
.

Given two manifolds
Y_{1}
and
Y_{2}
, by a
mixed product Poisson structure
on the product manifold
Y_{1}\times Y_{2}
we mean a Poisson bivector field
\pi
on
Y_{1}\times Y_{2}
that projects to well-defined Poisson structures on
Y_{1}
and
Y_{2}
. Given
Y_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq n
, where
n\geq 2
, a Poisson structure
\pi
on the product manifold

Y=Y_{1}\times\cdots\times Y_{n}
is said to be a
mixed product
if the projection of
\pi
to
Y_{i}\times Y_{j}
is a well-defined mixed product Poisson structure on
Y_{i}\times Y_{j}
for any
1\leq i<j\leq n
, and in this case, we also call the pair

(Y_{1}\times\cdots\times Y_{n},\pi)
a
mixed product Poisson manifold
.

\diamond

Assume now that
((G,{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle G}}),(G^{*},{\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}^{*}}}))
is any
pair of dual Poisson Lie groups, i.e. the Lie bialgebras
(\mathfrak{g},\delta_{\mathfrak{g}})
and
(\mathfrak{g}^{*},\delta_{\mathfrak{g}^{*}})

are dual to each other, where recall that
\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}:\mathfrak{g}\to\wedge^{2}\mathfrak{g}
and
\delta_{\mathfrak{g}^{*}}:\mathfrak{g}^{*}\to\wedge^{2}\mathfrak{g}^{*}

are respectively the linearizations of
{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle G}}
and
{\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}^{*}}}
at the identity elements of
G
and
G^{*}
.
Suppose that
(X,{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}})
and
(Y,{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle Y}})
are two Poisson manifolds, with respective
right
and
left

Poisson actions

\rho:\;(X,{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}})\times(G^{*},{\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}^{*}}})\longrightarrow(X,{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}})\hskip 14.45377pt\mbox{and}\hskip 14.45377pt\lambda:\;(G,{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle G}})\times(Y,{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle Y}})\longrightarrow(Y,{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle Y}})

(A.1)

by the Poisson Lie groups
(G^{*},\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}^{*}})
and
(G,\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle G})
. Let

\rho:\mathfrak{g}^{*}\to{\mathfrak{X}}^{1}(X)
and
\lambda:\mathfrak{g}\to{\mathfrak{X}}^{1}(Y)
be the corresponding Lie algebra homomorphism
and Lie algebra anti-homomorphism
(see notation in
\lx@sectionsign
1.6
). Then
\rho
and
\lambda
are respectively right and left Lie bialgebra actions (see

[
17
,
\lx@sectionsign
2.5]
).
Define the bi-vector field

{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}\times_{(\rho,\lambda)}{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle Y}}=({\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}},0)+(0,{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle Y}})-\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\rho(\xi_{i}^{*}),\,0)\wedge(0,\;\lambda(\xi_{i}))

(A.2)

on
X\times Y
,
where
\{\xi_{i}\}_{i=1,\ldots,n}
is any basis for
\mathfrak{g}
and

\{\xi_{i}^{*}\}_{i=1,\ldots,n}

its dual basis for
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
. Note that the definition of
{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}\times_{(\rho,\lambda)}{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle Y}}
only uses the Lie algebra actions
\rho
and
\lambda
.

Lemma-Definition A.2
.

[
17
, Proposition 4.2]
For any pair
(\rho,\lambda)
of Poisson Lie group actions in (
A.1
),
the bi-vector field
{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}\times_{(\rho,\lambda)}{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle Y}}
on
X\times Y
is Poisson, and it
is called the mixed product of
{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}
and
{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle Y}}
associated to the pair
(\rho,\lambda)
.

Example A.3
.

Let
(\rho,\lambda)
be a pair of Poisson Lie group actions as in (
A.1
). Equipping
G^{*}\times Y
with the
Poisson structure
\pi=\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}^{*}}\times_{(\rho_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}^{*}},\lambda)}{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle Y}}
, where
\rho_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}^{*}}
is the right action action of
G^{*}
on itself by right translation,
one then has the left Poisson Lie group action

\lambda^{\prime}:\;\;(G^{*},\;\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}^{*}})\times(G^{*}\times Y,\;\pi)\longrightarrow(G^{*}\times Y,\;\pi),\;(a,\,(a^{\prime},y))\longmapsto(aa^{\prime},y),\hskip 14.45377pta,a^{\prime}\in G^{*},\,y\in Y.

Let
X\times_{G^{*}}(G^{*}\times Y)
is the quotient of
X\times(G^{*}\times Y)
by the right diagonal action of
G^{*}
.
The Poisson structure
{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}\times\pi
on

X\times(G^{*}\times Y)
then projects to a well-defined Poisson structure on
X\times_{G^{*}}(G^{*}\times Y)
,
which we denote as
\pi^{\prime}
.
On the other hand, one has the
isomorphism

\psi:\;\;X\times_{G^{*}}(G^{*}\times Y)\longrightarrow X\times Y,\;[x,\,(a,y)]\longmapsto(xa,\,y),\;\hskip 14.45377ptx\in X,\,a\in G^{*},\,y\in Y.

By an argument similar to that used in the proof of
[
16
, Lemma A.1]
, one sees that

\psi:(X\times_{G^{*}}(G^{*}\times Y),\;\pi^{\prime})\longrightarrow(X\times Y,\;{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}\times_{(\rho,\lambda)}{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle Y}})

is a Poisson map.

\diamond

Consider now the standard complex semi-simple Lie group
(G,\pi_{\rm st})
, and we keep the notation set up in
\lx@sectionsign
3.1
.
Define the non-degenerate bilinear pairing
\langle\,,\,\rangle_{(\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{b}_{-})}
between
\mathfrak{b}
and
\mathfrak{b}_{-}
by

\langle x_{-}+x_{0},\;\,y_{+}+y_{0}\rangle_{(\mathfrak{b}_{-},\mathfrak{b})}=\frac{1}{2}\langle x_{-},\;y_{+}\rangle_{\mathfrak{g}}+\langle x_{0},\;y_{0}\rangle_{\mathfrak{g}},\hskip 14.45377ptx_{-}\in\mathfrak{n}_{-},\,\,x_{0},\,y_{0}\in\mathfrak{h},\,y_{+}\in\mathfrak{n}_{.}

(A.3)

For a basis
\{x_{i}\}
for
\mathfrak{b}_{-}
and dual basis
\{x^{i}\}
for
\mathfrak{b}
under
\langle\,,\,\rangle_{(\mathfrak{b}_{-},\mathfrak{b})}
, we choose (see
\lx@sectionsign
3.1
)

\displaystyle\{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\dim\mathfrak{b}_{-}}=\{h_{i}:1\leq i\leq\dim\mathfrak{h}\}\cup\{2E_{-\alpha}:\alpha\in\Delta^{+}\},

(A.4)

\displaystyle\{x^{i}\}_{i=1}^{\dim\mathfrak{b}}=\{h_{i}:1\leq i\leq\dim\mathfrak{h}\}\cup\{E_{\alpha}:\alpha\in\Delta^{+}\}.

(A.5)

We now give three pairs of Poisson Lie groups to be used to form mixed Poisson products.

Example A.4
.

With the non-degenerate pairing
\langle\,,\,\rangle_{(\mathfrak{b}_{-},\mathfrak{b})}
between Lie algebras
\mathfrak{b}_{-}
of
B_{-}
and
\mathfrak{b}
of
B
given in (
A.3
),
we have the first pair of dual Poisson Lie groups

((B_{-},\pi_{\rm st}),\;(B,-\pi_{\rm st}))
.
Under the same pairing
\langle\,,\,\rangle_{(\mathfrak{b}_{-},\mathfrak{b})}
, the Poisson Lie groups

(B_{-}^{\rm op},\pi_{\rm st})
and
(B,\pi_{\rm st})
are also dual to each other,
where
B_{-}^{\rm op}
is the manifold
B_{-}
with the group structure opposite to that on
B_{-}
.
A third pair of dual Poisson Lie groups is
((B_{-},-\pi_{\rm st}),(B,\pi_{\rm st}))
, with the pairing between the Lie algebras
\mathfrak{b}_{-}
and
\mathfrak{b}
given by

-\langle\,,\,\rangle_{(\mathfrak{b}_{-},\mathfrak{b})}
. Finally we also have the
pair of dual (direct product) Poisson Lie groups

(A,\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle A}})=(B_{-},\pi_{\rm st})\times(B_{-}^{\rm op},\pi_{\rm st}),\hskip 14.45377pt(A^{*},\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle A}^{*}})=(B,-\pi_{\rm st})\times(B,\pi_{\rm st}),

where the non-degenerate pairing between the Lie algebras
\mathfrak{a}=\mathfrak{b}_{-}\oplus\mathfrak{b}_{-}
and
\mathfrak{a}^{*}=\mathfrak{b}\oplus\mathfrak{b}
is the direct sum
of the pairing
\langle\,,\,\rangle_{(\mathfrak{b}_{-},\mathfrak{b})}
with itself.

\diamond

Example A.5
.

Consider the maximal torus
T
of
G
with the zero Poisson structure as a Poisson Lie group. By identifying
the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{h}
of
T
with
\mathfrak{h}^{*}
using the bilinear form
\langle\,,\,\rangle_{\mathfrak{g}}|_{\mathfrak{h}}
,
T
is also a dual Poisson Lie group of itself.
Let
(X,{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}})
be a
T
-Poisson manifold with
T
-action
\sigma:T\times X\to X
. The Poisson structure
{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}\bowtie_{\sigma}0
on
X\times T

given in (
1.9
) is then the mixed product Poisson structure defined using
(T,0)
as a Poisson Lie group.

\diamond

A.2.
The Poisson varieties
({\mathcal{C}}_{n},\widehat{\pi}_{n})
and
({\mathcal{B}}^{n},\overline{\pi}_{n})
as mixed products

Consider now the complex semi-simple Poisson Lie group
(G,\pi_{\rm st})
, and let the notation be as in
\lx@sectionsign
3.1
.
Let
Q
be any closed coisotropic subgroup of
(G,\pi_{\rm st})
, i.e.,
Q
is a closed Lie
subgroup of
G
which is also a coisotropic with respect to the Poisson structure
\pi_{\rm st}
. For an integer
n\geq 1
, let

F_{n,{\scriptscriptstyle Q}}=\overbrace{G\times_{B}\cdots\times_{B}G}^{n}/Q=\widetilde{F}_{n}/Q,

and denoted by
\pi_{n,{\scriptscriptstyle Q}}
the Poisson structure on
F_{n,{\scriptscriptstyle Q}}
that is the quotient of the Poisson structure
(\pi_{\rm st})^{n}
on
G^{n}
.
When
n=1
, the quotient Poisson structure on
G/Q
will be denoted as
\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}/{\scriptscriptstyle Q}}
. Note that when
Q=\{e\}
and when
Q=B
,
we respectively have
\pi_{n,{\scriptscriptstyle Q}}=\widetilde{\pi}_{n}
and
\pi_{n,{\scriptscriptstyle Q}}=\pi_{n}
. In particular,
the flag variety
{\mathcal{B}}:=G/B
has the Poisson structure
\pi_{1}=\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}/{\scriptscriptstyle B}}
, and the decorated flag variety
{\mathcal{A}}=G/N

has the Poisson structure
\widehat{\pi}_{1}:=\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}/{\scriptscriptstyle N}}
.

Returning to the case of an arbitrary closed coisotropic Lie subgroup
Q
of
(G,\pi_{\rm st})
, set

{\mathcal{Q}}_{n}={\mathcal{B}}^{n-1}\times(G/Q),\hskip 14.45377ptn\geq 1.

One then has the isomorphism

\Theta_{n,{\scriptscriptstyle Q}}:\;F_{n,{\scriptscriptstyle Q}}\to{\mathcal{Q}}_{n},\;[g_{1},\cdots,g_{n-1},g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}}\longmapsto(g_{1}{}_{\cdot}B,\ldots,g_{1}\cdots g_{n-1}{}_{\cdot}B,g_{1}\cdots g_{n-1}g_{n}{}_{\cdot}Q).

Define the Poisson structure
\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{Q}}_{n}}}
on
{\mathcal{Q}}_{n}
by

\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{Q}}_{n}}}\,=\,\Theta_{n,{\scriptscriptstyle Q}}(\pi_{n,{\scriptscriptstyle Q}}).

Our goal now is to express the Poisson
manifold
({\mathcal{Q}}_{n},\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{Q}}_{n}}})
as a mixed product of
n-1
copies of
({\mathcal{B}},\pi_{1})
with
(G/Q,\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}/{\scriptscriptstyle Q}})
.

Let
\lambda_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}/{\scriptscriptstyle Q}}
be the left Poisson action of
(G,\pi_{\rm st})
on
(G/Q,\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}/{\scriptscriptstyle Q}})
given by
translation, i.e,

\lambda_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}/{\scriptscriptstyle Q}}:\;(G,\pi_{\rm st})\times(G/Q,\,\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}/{\scriptscriptstyle Q}})\longrightarrow(G/Q,\,\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}/{\scriptscriptstyle Q}}),\;(g,\,g_{1}{}_{\cdot}Q)\longmapsto gg_{1}{}_{\cdot}Q.

Recall the bases
\{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\dim\mathfrak{b}_{-}}
of
\mathfrak{b}_{-}
and
\{x^{i}\}_{i=1}^{\dim\mathfrak{b}_{-}}
of
\mathfrak{b}
in (
A.4
).
For
1\leq j<k\leq n-1
, define
\mu_{j,k}^{(n-1)}\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{2}({\mathcal{B}}^{n-1})
by (see notation in
\lx@sectionsign
1.6
)

\mu_{j,k}^{(n-1)}=\sum_{i=1}^{\dim\mathfrak{b}_{-}}(0,\;\ldots,\;0,\;\underset{j^{\text{th}}\,\text{entry}}{\lambda_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}/{\scriptscriptstyle B}}(x^{i})},\;0,\;\ldots,\;0)\wedge(0,\;\ldots,\;0,\;\underset{k^{\text{th}}\,\text{entry}}{\lambda_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}/{\scriptscriptstyle B}}(x_{i})},\;0,\;\ldots,\;0),

and for
1\leq j\leq n-1
, define
\mu_{j}^{(n,{\scriptscriptstyle Q})}\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{2}({\mathcal{Q}}_{n})
by

\mu_{j}^{(n,{\scriptscriptstyle Q})}=\sum_{i=1}^{\dim\mathfrak{b}_{-}}(0,\;\ldots,\;0,\;\underset{j^{\text{th}}\,\text{entry}}{\lambda_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}/{\scriptscriptstyle B}}(x^{i})},\;0,\;\ldots,\;0)\wedge(0,\;\ldots,\;0,\;\lambda_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}/{\scriptscriptstyle Q}}(x_{i})).

Proposition A.6
.

For any closed coisotropic subgroup
Q
of
(G,\pi_{\rm st})
, one has

\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{Q}}_{n}}}=(\pi_{1},\,\ldots,\,\pi_{1},\,\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}/{\scriptscriptstyle Q}})+\sum_{1\leq j<k\leq n-1}(\mu_{j,k}^{(n-1)},\;0)+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\mu_{j}^{(n,{\scriptscriptstyle Q})}.

(A.6)

Proof.

Note first that the standard
r
-matrix
r_{\rm st}
on
\mathfrak{g}
given in (
3.1
) is given by

r_{\rm st}=\sum_{i=1}^{\dim\mathfrak{b}_{-}}x_{i}\otimes x^{i}\in\in\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}.

The case of
Q=\{e\}
follows from the proof of
[
17
, Proposition 8.1]
by setting, in the notation of
[
17
, Proposition 8.1]
,

Q_{+}=B
and
r=r_{\rm st}
.
The case for arbitrary
Q
then follows from the following commutative diagram of Poisson manifolds

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F_{n,\{e\}},\,\pi_{n,\{e\}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\mathcal{B}}^{n-1}\times G,\,\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{G}}_{n}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F_{n,{\scriptscriptstyle Q}},\,\pi_{n,{\scriptscriptstyle Q}})\;}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\;\;({\mathcal{Q}}_{n},\,\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{Q}}_{n}}}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{n,\{e\}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{n,{\scriptscriptstyle Q}}}

where the second vertical arrow is
{\rm Id}_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{B}}^{n-1}}}\times p_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}/{\scriptscriptstyle Q}}
, with
p_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}/{\scriptscriptstyle Q}}:G\to G/Q

the projection.

Q.E.D.

For the special case of
Q=B
, let
\overline{\pi}_{n}=\Theta_{n,{\scriptscriptstyle B}}(\pi_{n})
.

Corollary A.7
.

For any
n\geq 1
, the Poisson structure
\overline{\pi}_{n}
on
{\mathcal{B}}^{n}
is given by

\overline{\pi}_{n}=(\pi_{1},\pi_{1},\ldots,\pi_{1})+\sum_{1\leq j<k\leq n}\mu_{jk}^{(n)},

(A.7)

where for
1\leq j<k\leq n
,
\mu_{j,k}^{(n)}\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{2}({\mathcal{B}}^{n})
is given by

\mu_{j,k}^{(n)}=\sum_{i=1}^{\dim\mathfrak{b}_{-}}(0,\;\ldots,\;0,\;\underset{j^{\text{th}}\,\text{entry}}{\lambda_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}/{\scriptscriptstyle B}}(x^{i})},\;0,\;\ldots,\;0)\wedge(0,\;\ldots,\;0,\;\underset{k^{\text{th}}\,\text{entry}}{\lambda_{{\scriptscriptstyle G}/{\scriptscriptstyle B}}(x_{i})},\;0,\;\ldots,\;0).

Remark A.8
.

By (
A.6
),
\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{Q}}_{n}}}

is a mixed product Poisson structure on
{\mathcal{Q}}_{n}
in the sense of Definition
A.1
.
The Poisson structure
\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{Q}}_{n}}}
on
{\mathcal{Q}}_{n}={\mathcal{B}}^{n-1}\times G/Q
can also be written as two fold mixed products:
consider again the pair of dual Poisson Lie groups

((B_{-},\pi_{\rm st}),(B,-\pi_{\rm st}))
from Example
A.4
.
For
1\leq k\leq n-1
, define the right Poisson action
\tilde{\rho}_{k}
of
(B,-\pi_{\rm st})
on
{\mathcal{B}}^{k}
by

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}_{k}:

\displaystyle\;({\mathcal{B}}^{k},\overline{\pi}_{k})\times(B,-\pi_{\rm st})\longrightarrow({\mathcal{B}}^{k},\overline{\pi}_{k}),

\displaystyle\;((f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{k}),\,b)\longmapsto(b^{-1}f_{1},\,b^{-1}f_{2},\ldots,b^{-1}f_{k}),

and let
\lambda_{n-k,{\scriptscriptstyle Q}}
be the left Poisson action of
(B_{-},\pi_{\rm st})
on
({\mathcal{Q}}_{n-k},\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{Q}}_{n-k}}})
given by

\displaystyle\lambda_{n-k,{\scriptscriptstyle Q}}:

\displaystyle\;(B_{-},\pi_{\rm st})\times({\mathcal{Q}}_{n-k},\,\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{Q}}_{n-k}}})\longrightarrow({\mathcal{Q}}_{n-k},\,\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{Q}}_{n-k}}}),

\displaystyle\;(b_{-},\,(f_{1},\,\ldots,\,f_{n-k-1},\,g_{\cdot}Q))\longmapsto(b_{-}f_{1},\,\ldots,\,b_{-}f_{n-k-1},\,b_{-}g_{\cdot}Q)).

It then follows from Proposition
A.6
that

\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{Q}}_{n}}}=\overline{\pi}_{k}\times_{(\tilde{\rho}_{k},\lambda_{n-k,{\scriptscriptstyle Q}})}\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{Q}}_{n-k}}}.

See
[
17
, Remark 6.10]

for the general setting. In particular,
setting
\widehat{\pi}_{n}=\pi_{n,{\scriptscriptstyle Q}}
for
Q=N
, the Poisson structure
\widehat{\pi}_{n}
on
{\mathcal{B}}^{n-1}\times{\mathcal{A}}
then satisfies

\widehat{\pi}_{n}=\overline{\pi}_{k}\times_{(\tilde{\rho}_{k},\lambda_{n-k,{\scriptscriptstyle N}})}\widehat{\pi}_{n-k},\hskip 14.45377pt1\leq k\leq n-1.

(A.8)

\diamond

Appendix B
The Poisson isomorphism
J_{n}

B.1.
The isomorphism
J_{n}

For
n\geq 1
, recall the Poisson action

\lambda_{n}:\;(G,\pi_{\rm st})\times(F_{n},\pi_{n})\longrightarrow(F_{n},\pi_{n}),\;\;\lambda_{n}(g,[g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}})=[gg_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}},

which in particular makes
(F_{n},\pi_{n})
into a
T
-Poisson manifold. By (
1.9
),
one has the
T
-extension

(F_{n}\times T,\,\pi_{n}\bowtie_{\lambda_{n}}0)
of
(F_{n},\pi_{n})
.
For notational simplicity, we set

\pi_{n}\bowtie 0=\pi_{n}\bowtie_{\lambda_{n}}0=(\pi_{n},0)+\sum_{i=1}^{\dim\mathfrak{h}}(\lambda_{n}(h_{i}),0)\wedge(0,h_{i}^{R})\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{2}(F_{n}\times T).

Recall that the open sub-manifold
F_{n}^{o}
of
F_{n}
is defined as

F_{n}^{o}=\{[g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}}:g_{1}g_{2}\cdots g_{n}\in B_{-}\},

and that we have the
T
-equivariant isomorphism

J_{n}:\Gamma_{n}\to F_{n}^{o}\times T
(see (
3.14
)) given by

J_{n}([g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}})=([g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}},\,[g_{1}g_{2}\cdots g_{n}]_{0}),

where
T
acts on
F_{n}^{o}\times T
diagonally.
In this section we prove the following fact also stated as Theorem
3.5
.

Theorem B.1
.

For any
n\geq 1
,
J_{n}:(\Gamma_{n},\,\widetilde{\pi}_{n})\to(F_{n}^{o}\times T,\,\pi_{n}\bowtie 0)
is a Poisson isomorphism.

B.2.
Some auxiliary lemmas

Consider the pair of dual Poisson Lie groups
((B_{-},\pi_{\rm st}),(B,-\pi_{\rm st}))
from Example
A.4
.
The left Poisson action
\lambda_{n}
of
(G,\pi_{\rm st})
on
(F_{n},\pi_{n})

restricts to a left Poisson action of
(B_{-},\pi_{\rm st})
on
(F_{n},\pi_{n})
, still denoted by
\lambda_{n}
. One also has the induced

right
Poisson action of
(B,-\pi_{\rm st})
on
(F_{n},\pi_{n})
by

\displaystyle\rho_{n}:\;

\displaystyle(F_{n},\,\pi_{n})\times(B,-\pi_{\rm st})\longrightarrow(F_{n},\pi_{n}),

(B.1)

\displaystyle([g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}},\,b)\longmapsto[b^{-1}g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}}.

Recall that
\lambda_{n}
also denotes the induced Lie algebra anti-homomorphism
\lambda_{n}:\mathfrak{g}\to{\mathfrak{X}}^{1}(F_{n})
, and recall that
for
x\in\mathfrak{g}
,
x^{R}
is the right invariant vector field on
G
with value
x

at the identity element
e
. For every sub-manifold
S
of
G
that is invariant under left translation by elements in
B_{-}
, we have
the Lie algebra anti-homomorphism

\lambda_{\mathfrak{b}_{-}}:\mathfrak{b}_{-}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}^{1}(S),\;\;\lambda_{\mathfrak{b}_{-}}(x)=x^{R}|_{{\scriptscriptstyle S}}.

We further simplify notation as follows.

Notation B.2
.

If
(X,{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}})
is a Poisson manifold with a right Poisson action

\rho:\;(X,{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}})\times(B,-\pi_{\rm st})\longrightarrow(X,{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}),\;\;(x,b)\longmapsto xb,

(B.2)

of the Poisson Lie group
(B,-\pi_{\rm st})
, we set

\displaystyle{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}\bowtie_{\rho}\pi_{\rm st}

\displaystyle={\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}\times_{(\rho,\lambda_{\mathfrak{b}_{-}})}\pi_{\rm st}=({\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}},0)+(0,\pi_{\rm st})-\sum_{i=1}^{\dim\mathfrak{b}_{-}}(\rho(x^{i}),0)\wedge(0,x_{i}^{R})\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{2}(X\times G),

\displaystyle{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}\bowtie_{\rho}\pi_{n}

\displaystyle={\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}\times_{(\rho,\lambda_{n})}\pi_{\rm st}=({\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}},0)+(0,\pi_{n})-\sum_{i=1}^{\dim\mathfrak{b}_{-}}(\rho(x^{i}),0)\wedge(0,\lambda_{n}(x_{i}))\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{2}(X\times F_{n})

for
n\geq 1
, where
\{x^{i}\}
and
\{x_{i}\}
are given in (
A.4
). In the special case of
(X,{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}})=(F_{m},\pi_{m})

and
\rho=\rho_{m}
for
m\geq 1
as in
(
B.1
), we further simplify the notation to set

\displaystyle\pi_{m}\bowtie\pi_{\rm st}

\displaystyle=\pi_{m}\bowtie_{\rho_{m}}\pi_{\rm st}=(\pi_{m},0)+(0,\pi_{\rm st})+\sum_{i=1}^{\dim\mathfrak{b}_{-}}(\lambda_{m}(x^{i}),0)\wedge(0,x_{i}^{R})\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{2}(F_{m}\times G),\hskip 14.45377pt

\displaystyle\pi_{m}\bowtie\pi_{n}

\displaystyle=\pi_{m}\bowtie_{\rho_{m}}\pi_{n}=(\pi_{m},0)+(0,\pi_{n})+\sum_{i=1}^{\dim\mathfrak{b}_{-}}(\lambda_{m}(x^{i}),0)\wedge(0,\lambda_{n}(x_{i}))\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{2}(F_{m}\times F_{n}).

\diamond

For
m,n\geq 1
, consider the isomorphism
\Theta_{m,n}:F_{m+n}\to F_{m}\times F_{n}
given by

\Theta_{m,n}([g_{1},\ldots,g_{m+n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{m+n}})=([g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{m}},\,[g_{1}\cdots g_{m}g_{m+1},\,g_{m+2},\,\ldots,\,g_{m+n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}}.

(B.3)

Consider also the isomorphism

\widetilde{\Theta}_{n}:\;\widetilde{F}_{n}\longrightarrow F_{n-1}\times G,\;[g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}}\longmapsto([g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}},\;g_{1}g_{2}\cdots g_{n}).

(B.4)

The following lemma follows direct from Remark
A.8
.

Lemma B.3
.

For
m,n\geq 1
, one has

\Theta_{m,n}(\pi_{m+n})=\pi_{m}\bowtie\pi_{n}\hskip 14.45377pt\mbox{and}\hskip 14.45377pt\widetilde{\Theta}_{n}(\widetilde{\pi}_{n})=\pi_{n-1}\bowtie\pi_{\rm st}.

Assume that
(X,{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}})
is a Poisson manifold with a right Poisson action
\rho
of the Poisson Lie group
(B,-\pi_{\rm st})
as in (
B.2
),
and consider the isomorphism

I_{\scriptscriptstyle X}:\;\;X\times B_{-}\longrightarrow(X\times F_{1}^{o})\times T,\;\;(x,\,b_{-})\longmapsto(x,\;b_{-}{}_{\cdot}B,\;[b_{-}]_{0}).

(B.5)

Equip
X\times B_{-}
and
X\times F_{1}^{o}
with the Poisson structures
{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}\bowtie_{\rho}\pi_{\rm st}
and
{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}\bowtie_{\rho}\pi_{1}
,
respectively.
Equip
X\times F_{1}^{o}
with the action of
T
by

\sigma_{1}:\;T\times(X\times F_{1}^{o})\longrightarrow X\times F_{1}^{o},\;\;(t,\,(x,\,g_{\cdot}B))\longmapsto(xt^{-1},\;tg_{\cdot}B),

(B.6)

which preserves the Poisson structure
{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}\bowtie_{\rho}\pi_{1}
, so one has the
T
-extension Poisson structure

({\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}\bowtie_{\rho}\pi_{1})\bowtie_{\sigma_{1}}0
on
(X\times F_{1})\times T
.

Lemma B.4
.

The map
I_{\scriptscriptstyle X}:(X\times B_{-},\,{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}\bowtie_{\rho}\pi_{\rm st})\to((X\times F_{1}^{o})\times T,\,({\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}\bowtie_{\rho}\pi_{1})\bowtie_{\sigma_{1}}0)

is a Poisson isomorphism.

Proof.

Consider the isomorphism

I_{0}:\;B_{-}\longrightarrow F_{1}^{o}\times T,\;\;b_{-}\longmapsto(b_{-}{}_{\cdot}B,\,[b_{-}]_{0}),\hskip 14.45377ptb_{-}\in B_{-}.

By
[
16
, Proposition A.7]
,
I_{0}:(B_{-},\;\pi_{\rm st})\to(F_{1}^{o}\times T,\,\pi_{1}\bowtie 0)
is Poisson, where

\pi_{1}\bowtie 0=(\pi_{1},0)+\sum_{i=1}^{\dim\mathfrak{h}}(\lambda_{1}(h_{i}),0)\wedge(0,h_{i}^{R}).

Note that
I_{\scriptscriptstyle X}={\rm Id}_{\scriptscriptstyle X}\times I_{0}
. Using the bases
\{x^{i}\}
of
\mathfrak{b}
and
\{x_{i}\}
of
\mathfrak{b}_{-}
in (
A.4
),

{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}\bowtie_{\rho}\pi_{\rm st}=({\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}},0)+(0,\pi_{\rm st})-2\sum_{\alpha\in\Delta^{+}}(\rho(E_{\alpha}),0)\wedge(0,E_{-\alpha}^{R})-\sum_{i=1}^{\dim\mathfrak{h}}(\rho(h_{i}),0)\wedge(0,h_{i}^{R}).

It follows that as bivector fields on
X\times F_{1}^{o}\times T
,

\displaystyle I_{\scriptscriptstyle X}({\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}\bowtie_{\rho}\pi_{\rm st})

\displaystyle=({\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}},0,0)+(0,\pi_{1},0)+\sum_{i=1}^{\dim\mathfrak{h}}(0,\,\lambda_{1}(h_{i}),\,0)\wedge(0,\,0,\,h_{i}^{R})

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;-2\sum_{\alpha\in\Delta^{+}}(\rho(E_{\alpha}),0,0)\wedge(0,\lambda_{1}(E_{-\alpha}),0)-\sum_{i=1}^{\dim\mathfrak{h}}(\rho(h_{i}),0,0)\wedge(0,\lambda_{1}(h_{i}),h_{i}^{R}).

On the other hand, by (
B.6
),

\displaystyle({\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}\bowtie_{\rho}\pi_{1})\bowtie_{\sigma}0

\displaystyle=({\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}},0,0)+(0,\pi_{1},0)-2\sum_{\alpha\in\Delta^{+}}(\rho(E_{\alpha}),0,0)\wedge(0,\lambda_{1}(E_{{}_{\alpha}}),0)

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;-\sum_{i=1}^{\dim\mathfrak{h}}(\rho(h_{i}),0,0)\wedge(0,\lambda_{1}(h_{i}),0)+\sum_{i=1}^{\dim\mathfrak{h}}(-\rho(h_{i}),\lambda_{1}(h_{i}),0)\wedge(0,0,h_{i}^{R}).

Comparing terms, one sees that
I_{\scriptscriptstyle X}({\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}\bowtie_{\rho}\pi_{\rm st})=({\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle X}}\bowtie_{\rho}\pi_{1})\bowtie_{\sigma_{1}}0
.

Q.E.D.

B.3.
Proof of Theorem
B.1

Since

J_{n}=\Theta_{n-1,2}\circ I_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n-1}}\circ\widetilde{\Theta}_{n}
, and

\displaystyle(\Gamma,\,\widetilde{\pi}_{n})\;

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widetilde{\Theta}_{n}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\;(F_{n-1}\times B_{-},\;\pi_{n-1}\bowtie\pi_{\rm st})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle I_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n-1}}}}{{-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow}}\;((F_{n-1}\times F_{1}^{o})\times T,\;(\pi_{n-1}\bowtie\pi_{1})\bowtie_{\sigma_{1}}0)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Theta_{n-1,1}^{-1}\times{\rm Id}_{T}}}{{-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow}}\;(F_{n}^{o}\times T,\;\pi_{n}\bowtie 0),

are Poisson maps, respectively by Lemma
B.3
, Lemma
B.4
, and Lemma
B.3
again.
we see that
J_{n}:(\Gamma_{n},\widetilde{\pi}_{n})\to(F_{n}^{o}\times T,\,\pi_{n}\bowtie 0)
is Poisson.

This finishes the proof of Theorem
B.1
.

Appendix C
Generalized double Bruhat cells

Generalizing the case of
m=n
from
[
18
,
\lx@sectionsign
1.4]
,
we introduce in this appendix the
T
-Poisson manifold
(G_{m,n},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})
, for any pair of integers
m,n\geq 1
,
and the
generalized double Bruhat cells

G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}
as its
T
-leaves, where
({\bf u},{\bf v})\in W^{m}\times W^{n}
.
The main results of this appendix are presented in
\lx@sectionsign
C.2
, where we establish, using any
representative
\dot{{\bf v}}\in{\bf v}T^{n}
for each
{\bf v}\in W^{n}
, a piece-wise
Poisson isomorphism from

(G_{m,n},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})
to
(\Gamma_{m+n},\widetilde{\pi}_{m+n})
and thus also to
(F_{m+n}^{o}\times T,\pi_{m+n}\bowtie 0)
. These
piece-wise Poisson isomorphisms carry
T
-leaves to
T
-leaves, giving rise in particular to
Poisson isomorphisms (see Corollary
C.8
)

K_{{\bf u},\dot{{\bf v}}}:\;(G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left({\mathcal{O}}^{({\bf u},{\bf v}^{-1})}_{e}\times T,\,\pi_{m+n}\bowtie 0\right).

As a special case, we show in Corollary
C.9
that each
(\widetilde{F}_{n},\widetilde{\pi}_{n})
is also piece-wise Poisson isomorphic to

(F_{n+1}^{o}\times T,\pi_{n+1}\bowtie 0)
. Consequently every
T
-leaf of

(\widetilde{F}_{n},\widetilde{\pi}_{n})
is also Poisson isomorphic to
({\mathcal{O}}^{{\bf w}}_{e}\times T,\pi_{n+1}\bowtie 0)
for some
{\bf w}\in W^{n+1}
.

C.1.
Generalized double Bruhat cells associated to conjugacy classes

Recall from
\lx@sectionsign
4.3
that for an integer
n\geq 1
, we have the quotient space

\widetilde{F}_{-n}=G\times_{B_{-}}\cdots\times_{B_{-}}G

with the well-defined quotient Poisson structure
\widetilde{\pi}_{-n}
, and that the image of

(g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n})\in G^{n}
in
\widetilde{F}_{-n}
is denoted as
[g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{-n}}
. Similar to the case of
(\widetilde{F}_{n},\widetilde{\pi}_{n})
,

(\widetilde{F}_{-n},\widetilde{\pi}_{-n})
is a
T
-Poisson manifold with the
T
-action given by

t\cdot[g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{-n}}=[tg_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{-n}}.

(C.1)

Recall from Example
A.4
that we have the
pair of dual Poisson Lie groups

(A,\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle A}})=(B_{-},\pi_{\rm st})\times(B_{-}^{\rm op},\pi_{\rm st}),\hskip 14.45377pt(A^{*},\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle A}^{*}})=(B,-\pi_{\rm st})\times(B,\pi_{\rm st}).

As the Poisson structure
\pi_{\rm st}
on
G
is multiplicative, one has the right and left Poisson actions

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}_{n}:\;(\widetilde{F}_{n},\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\widetilde{F}}}_{n}})\times(A^{*},\,\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle A}^{*}})\longrightarrow(\widetilde{F}_{n},\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\widetilde{F}}}_{n}}),

\displaystyle\tilde{\lambda}_{-n}:\;(A,\,\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle A}})\times(\widetilde{F}_{-n},\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\widetilde{F}}}_{-n}})\longrightarrow(\widetilde{F}_{-n},\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\widetilde{F}}}_{-n}})

of the Poisson Lie groups
(A,{\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle G}})
and
(A^{*},\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle A}^{*}})
,
respectively given by

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}_{n}([g_{1},\,\ldots,\,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\widetilde{F}}}_{n}},\;(b_{1},b_{2}))=[b_{1}^{-1}g_{1},\,g_{2},\ldots,\,g_{n-1},\,g_{n}b_{2}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\widetilde{F}}}_{n}},

(C.2)

\displaystyle\tilde{\lambda}_{-n}((b_{-1},b_{-2}),\;[g_{1},\,\ldots,\,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\widetilde{F}}}_{-n}})=[b_{-1}g_{1},\,g_{2},\ldots,\,g_{n-1},\,g_{n}b_{-2}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\widetilde{F}}}_{-n}},

(C.3)

where
g_{j}\in G
for each
j\in[1,n]
, and
b_{1},b_{2}\in B,\,b_{-1},b_{-2}\in B_{-}
.

Definition C.1
.

For integers
m,n\geq 1
,
the Poisson structure
\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n}
on
\widetilde{F}_{m}\times\widetilde{F}_{-n}
is defined to be the
mixed product

\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n}=\widetilde{\pi}_{m}\times_{(\tilde{\rho}_{m},\tilde{\lambda}_{-n})}\widetilde{\pi}_{-n}.

Note that the Poisson structure
\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n}
is invariant under the diagonal
T
-action on
\widetilde{F}_{m}\times\widetilde{F}_{-n}
.

Notation C.2
.

For a conjugacy class
C
in
G
, let

G_{m,n,{\scriptscriptstyle C}}=\{([g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\widetilde{F}}}_{m}},\,[k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle{\widetilde{F}}}_{-n}}):g_{1}\cdots g_{m}(k_{1}\cdots k_{n})^{-1}\in C\}\subset\widetilde{F}_{m}\times\widetilde{F}_{-n},

and for
{\bf u}=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{m})\in W^{m}
and
{\bf v}=(v_{1},\ldots,v_{n})\in W^{n}
, let

G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle C}}=G_{m,n,{\scriptscriptstyle C}}\cap\left((B{\bf u}B)\times B_{-}{\bf v}B_{-})\right)\subset\widetilde{F}_{m}\times\widetilde{F}_{-n}.

We will refer to
G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle C}}
as the
generalized double Bruhat cell associated to the conjugacy
class
C
and the sequences
{\bf u}
and
{\bf v}
.
One thus has the decomposition of
\widetilde{F}_{m}\times\widetilde{F}_{-n}
into the disjoint union

\widetilde{F}_{m}\times\widetilde{F}_{-n}=\bigsqcup_{{\bf u},{\bf v},{\scriptscriptstyle C}}G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle C}},

(C.4)

where
{\bf u}\in W^{m},{\bf v}\in W^{n}
, and
C
runs over the set of all conjugacy classes in
G
.

Theorem C.3
.

1) For any conjugacy class
C
in
G
and any
{\bf u}\in W^{m},{\bf v}\in W^{n}
,
G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle C}}
is
a non-empty connected smooth sub-manifold of
\widetilde{F}_{m}\times\widetilde{F}_{-n}
of dimension
l({\bf u})+l({\bf v})+\dim C+\dim T
;

2) The decomposition (
C.4
) is that of
\widetilde{F}_{m}\times\widetilde{F}_{-n}
into the

T
-leaves of
\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n}
for the
T
-action given in (
4.27
).

Proof.

The case when
m=n
is proved in
[
18
,
\lx@sectionsign
1.5]
. Let
m
and
n
be arbitrary,
and define
Z_{m}\subset\widetilde{F}_{m+n}
and
Z_{-n}\subset\widetilde{F}_{-(m+n)}
by

Z_{m}=\overbrace{{G\times_{B}}\cdots\times_{B}G}^{m\;\mbox{copies of}\;G}\times_{B}\overbrace{{B\times_{B}\cdots\times_{B}B}}^{n\;\mbox{copies of}\;B},\hskip 14.45377ptZ_{-n}=\overbrace{B_{-}\times_{B_{-}}\cdots\times_{B_{-}}B_{-}}^{m\;\mbox{copies of}\;B_{-}}\times_{B_{-}}\overbrace{{G\times_{B_{-}}\cdots\times_{B_{-}}G}}^{n\;\mbox{copies of}\;G}.

By the definition of the Poisson structure
\widetilde{\pi}_{m+n,m+n}
,

Z_{m}\times Z_{-n}
is a Poisson sub-manifold of
(\widetilde{F}_{m+n}\times\widetilde{F}_{-(m+n)},\widetilde{\pi}_{m+n,m+n})
.
Define

\mu:\widetilde{F}_{m+n}\to\widetilde{F}_{m}
and
\mu_{-}:\widetilde{F}_{-(m+n)}\to\widetilde{F}_{-n}
by

\displaystyle\mu([g_{1},\ldots,\,g_{m-1},\,g_{m},\,g_{m+1},\,\ldots,\,g_{m+n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m+n}})=[g_{1},\,\ldots,\,g_{m-1},\,g_{m}g_{m+1}\cdots g_{m+n}]_{\widetilde{F}_{m}},

\displaystyle\mu_{-}([g_{1},\ldots,\,g_{m},\,g_{m+1},\,\ldots,\,g_{m+n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{-(m+n)}})=[g_{1}\cdots g_{m}g_{m+1},\,g_{m+2},\,\ldots,\,g_{m+n}]_{\widetilde{F}_{-n}}.

By Example
A.3
, one sees that

\phi:=\mu|_{{\scriptscriptstyle Z}_{m}}\times\mu_{-}|_{{\scriptscriptstyle Z}_{-n}}:\;(Z_{m}\times Z_{-n},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})\longrightarrow(\widetilde{F}_{m}\times\widetilde{F}_{-n},\,\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})

is a
T
-equivariant Poisson isomorphism.
The statement on the
T
-leaf decomposition of
(\widetilde{F}_{m}\times\widetilde{F}_{-n},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})
now follows from that for

(\widetilde{F}_{m+n}\times\widetilde{F}_{-(m+n)},\widetilde{\pi}_{m+n,m+n})

given in
[
18
, Theorem 1.4]
.

Q.E.D.

Specializing to the case when
C=\{e\}
is the trivial conjugacy class, we have the Poisson sub-manifold
G_{m,n}
of
(\widetilde{F}_{m}\times\widetilde{F}_{-n},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})
.,
where, we have already introduced in
\lx@sectionsign
4.3
,

G_{m,n}=G_{m,n,\{e\}}=\{([g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{m}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m}},[h_{1},h_{2},\ldots,h_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}}):g_{1}g_{2}\cdots g_{n}=h_{1}h_{2}\cdots h_{n}\}.

The restriction of
\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n}
to
G_{m,n}
will still be denoted as
\pi_{m,n}
.
Again as we have done in
\lx@sectionsign
4.3
, for
{\bf u}\in W^{m}
and
{\bf v}\in W^{n}
,

G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}=G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}_{\{e\}}\subset G_{m,n}

is called a
generalized double Bruhat cell
.
We now have the following
Corollary
C.4

which generalizes the corresponding statements in
[
8
]
on for double Bruhat cells in
G
.

Corollary C.4
.

1) For any
{\bf u}\in W^{m},{\bf v}\in W^{n}
, the generalized double Bruhat cell

G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}
is
a non-empty connected sub-manifold of
G_{m,n}
of dimension
l({\bf u})+l({\bf v})+\dim T
;

2) the
T
-leaves of
(G_{m,n},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})
are
precisely the generalized double Bruhat cells
G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}
, where
{\bf u}\in W^{m}
and
{\bf v}\in W^{n}
.

C.2.
Piece-wise Poisson isomorphisms from
(G_{m,n},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})
to
(\Gamma_{m+n},\widetilde{\pi}_{m+n})

Fix integers
m,n\geq 1
.
Recall that the maximal torus
T
acts on both
G_{m,n}
and
\Gamma_{m+n}
, respectively via (
4.27
) and (
1.3
).
Recall also that we have set

{\bf v}^{-1}=(v_{n}^{-1},\ldots,v_{2}^{-1},v_{1}^{-1})\hskip 14.45377pt\mbox{if}\hskip 14.45377pt{\bf v}=(v_{1},v_{2},\ldots,v_{n})\in W^{n}.

Writing an arbitrary
{\bf w}\in W^{m+n}
as
{\bf w}=({\bf u},{\bf v}^{-1})
with

{\bf u}\in W^{m}
and
{\bf v}\in W^{n}
, by Proposition
4.1
, the
T
-leaf decomposition of
(\Gamma_{m+n},\widetilde{\pi}_{m+n})
can be re-written as

\Gamma_{m+n}=\bigsqcup_{{\bf u}\in W^{m},{\bf v}\in W^{n}}\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf v}^{-1})},

where recall that
\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf v}^{-1})}=\Gamma_{m+n}\cap(B({\bf u},{\bf v}^{-1})B)
.
For
{\bf v}\in W^{n}
, we set

G_{m,n}^{{\bf v}}=G_{m,n}\cap(\widetilde{F}_{m}\times(B_{-}{\bf v}B_{-})\hskip 14.45377pt\mbox{and}\hskip 14.45377pt\Gamma^{{\bf v}}_{m+n}=\Gamma_{m+n}\cap(\widetilde{F}_{m}\times_{B}(B{\bf v}^{-1}B)).

Here
\widetilde{F}_{m}\times_{B}(B{\bf v}^{-1}B)
is the quotient of
\widetilde{F}_{m}\times(B{\bf v}^{-1}B)
by the diagonal
B
-action

([g_{1},\ldots,g_{m-1},g_{m}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m}},\;g)\cdot b=([g_{1},\ldots,g_{m-1},g_{m}b]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m}},\;b^{-1}g).

See Example
2.4
for the notation
X\times_{B}Y
for manifolds
X
with a right
B
-action and
Y
with a left
B
-action.
Both
G_{m,n}^{{\bf v}}
and
\Gamma_{m+n}^{{\bf v}}
are then unions of
T
-leaves in the respective Poisson manifolds

(G_{m,n},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})

and
(\Gamma_{m+n},\widetilde{\pi}_{m+n})
, and one has the disjoint unions

G_{m,n}=\bigsqcup_{{\bf v}\in W^{n}}G_{m,n}^{{\bf v}}\hskip 14.45377pt\mbox{and}\hskip 14.45377pt\Gamma_{m+n}=\bigsqcup_{{\bf v}\in W^{n}}\Gamma_{m+n}^{{\bf v}}.

For
{\bf v}=(v_{1},\ldots,v_{n})\in W^{n}
and any
representative
\dot{{\bf v}}=({\dot{v}}_{1},\ldots,{\dot{v}}_{n})\in{\bf v}T^{n}
, recall that

C_{\dot{{\bf v}}}=C_{{\dot{v}}_{1}}\times\cdots\times C_{{\dot{v}}_{n}},\hskip 14.45377pt\mbox{where}\;\;C_{{\dot{v}}_{j}}=N{\dot{v}}_{j}\cap{\dot{v}}_{j}N_{-}\;\;\mbox{for}\;\;1\leq j\leq n.

Denoting an arbitrary element in
C_{\dot{{\bf v}}}
as

\dot{c}=(\dot{c}_{1},\dot{c}_{2},\ldots,\dot{c}_{n})
and recalling from (
4.32
) and (
4.25
) the isomorphisms,

\displaystyle B_{-}\times C_{\dot{{\bf v}}}\longrightarrow B_{-}{\bf v}B_{-},\;\;(b_{-},\,\dot{c}_{1},\,\dot{c}_{2},\,\ldots,\,\dot{c}_{n})\longmapsto[b_{-}\dot{c}_{1},\,\dot{c}_{2},\,\ldots,\,\dot{c}_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{-n}},

\displaystyle B\times C_{\dot{{\bf v}}}\longrightarrow B{\bf v}^{-1}B,\;\;(b,\,\dot{c}_{1},\,\ldots,\,\dot{c}_{n-1},\,\dot{c}_{n})\longmapsto[b\dot{c}_{n}^{-1},\dot{c}_{n-1}^{-1},\,\ldots,\,\dot{c}_{1}^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}},

we have the well-defined map

\displaystyle E_{m,\dot{{\bf v}}}:

\displaystyle\;\;\widetilde{F}_{m}\times(B_{-}{\bf v}B_{-})\longrightarrow\widetilde{F}_{m}\times_{B}(B{\bf v}^{-1}B),

(C.5)

\displaystyle\;\;([g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m}},[b_{-}\dot{c}_{1},\dot{c}_{2},\ldots,\dot{c}_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{-n}})\longmapsto[g_{1},\ldots,g_{m},\,\dot{c}_{n}^{-1},\ldots,\,\dot{c}_{2}^{-1},\,\dot{c}_{1}^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m+n}}.

Note that
\widetilde{F}_{m}\times(B_{-}{\bf v}B_{-})
is a Poisson sub-manifold of
(\widetilde{F}_{m}\times\widetilde{F}_{-n},\,\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})
, and

\widetilde{F}_{m}\times_{B}(B{\bf v}^{-1}B)
is a Poisson sub-manifold of
(\widetilde{F}_{m+n},\,\widetilde{\pi}_{m+n})
.

Theorem C.5
.

For any
m\geq 0
, any
{\bf v}\in W^{n}
and any

\dot{{\bf v}}\in{\bf v}T^{n}
, the map

E_{m,\dot{{\bf v}}}:\;(\widetilde{F}_{m}\times(B_{-}{\bf v}B_{-}),\,\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})\longrightarrow(\widetilde{F}_{m}\times_{B}(B{\bf v}^{-1}B),\,\widetilde{\pi}_{m+n})

is Poisson and restricts to a Poisson isomorphism from
(G_{m,n}^{{\bf v}},\,\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})
to
(\Gamma_{m+n}^{{\bf v}},\,\widetilde{\pi}_{m+n})
.

Theorem
C.5
will be proved in
\lx@sectionsign
C.3
. We first give some consequences of Theorem
C.5
.

Remark C.6
.

The isomorphism
E_{m,\dot{{\bf v}}}
depends on the representative
\dot{{\bf v}}
of
{\bf v}
,
as indicated in the notation.
For a different choice
\dot{{\bf v}}^{\prime}
, one has
E_{m,\dot{{\bf v}}^{\prime}}=r_{t}\circ E_{m,\dot{{\bf v}}}
for some
t\in T
, where

r_{t}:\;\Gamma_{m+n}\longrightarrow\Gamma_{m+n},\;[g_{1},\,\ldots,\,g_{m+n-1},\,g_{m+n}]_{\widetilde{F}_{m+n}}\longmapsto[g_{1},\,\ldots,\,g_{m+n-1},\,g_{m+n}t]_{\widetilde{F}_{m+n}}.

For
m=n=1
,
G_{m,n}\cong G
and
\Gamma_{2}\cong{\mathcal{B}}\times B_{-}
via
[g_{1},g_{2}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{2}}\to(g_{1}{}_{\cdot}B,g_{1}g_{2})
.
The piece-wise isomorphisms from
G
to
{\mathcal{B}}\times B_{-}
have already been observed in
[
19
, Remark 10]
.
See

[
19
, Example 2]
for concrete calculations for the case of
G=SL(2,{\mathbb{C}})
.

\diamond

For
{\bf u}\in W^{m},{\bf v}\in W^{n}
and
\dot{{\bf v}}\in{\bf v}T^{n}
, set now

E_{{\bf u},\dot{{\bf v}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm def}}}{{=}}E_{m,\dot{{\bf v}}}|_{G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}}:\;\;G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}\longrightarrow\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf v}^{-1})}.

(C.6)

Corollary C.7
.

The map
E_{{\bf u},\dot{{\bf v}}}
gives a
T
-equivariant
Poisson isomorphism

E_{{\bf u},\dot{{\bf v}}}:\;\;(G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})\longrightarrow(\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf v}^{-1})},\widetilde{\pi}_{m+n})\subset(\Gamma_{m+n},\widetilde{\pi}_{m+n}).

Proof.

We have already seen that
E_{{\bf u},\dot{{\bf v}}}:G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}\to\Gamma_{m+n}
is
T
-equivariant,
and it is clear that
E_{{\bf u},\dot{{\bf v}}}(G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}})\subset\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf v}^{-1})}
. We show that

E_{{\bf u},\dot{{\bf v}}}
is an isomorphism by
writing down its inverse. By (
4.23
) and (
4.25
), one has the isomorphism

B{\bf u}B\times C_{\dot{{\bf v}}}\to B({\bf u},{\bf v}^{-1})B
given by

([g_{1},\ldots,g_{m-1},g_{m}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}},\dot{c}_{1},\ldots,\dot{c}_{n-1},\,\dot{c}_{n})\longmapsto[g_{1},\ldots,g_{m-1},g_{m},\,\dot{c}_{n}^{-1},\,\dot{c}_{n-1}^{-1},\,\ldots,\,\dot{c}_{1}^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m+n}}.

We thus have

\displaystyle(E_{{\bf u},\dot{{\bf v}}})^{-1}:\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf v}^{-1})}\to G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}},\;\;

\displaystyle[g_{1},\ldots,g_{m-1},g_{m},\,\dot{c}_{n}^{-1},\,\dot{c}_{n-1}^{-1},\,\ldots,\,\dot{c}_{1}^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m+n}}

\displaystyle\longmapsto([g_{1},\ldots,g_{m-1},g_{m}]_{\widetilde{F}_{m}},\;[b_{-}\dot{c}_{1},\,\ldots,\,\dot{c}_{n-1},\,\dot{c}_{n}]_{\widetilde{F}_{-n}}),

where
[g_{1},\ldots,g_{m-1},g_{m}]_{\widetilde{F}_{m}}\in B{\bf u}B
and
(\dot{c}_{1},\,\ldots,\,\dot{c}_{n-1},\,\dot{c}_{n})\in C_{\dot{{\bf v}}}
are such that

g_{1}\cdots g_{m-1}g_{m}\,\dot{c}_{n}^{-1}\,\dot{c}_{n-1}^{-1}\cdots\dot{c}_{1}^{-1}=b_{-}\in B_{-}.

Q.E.D.

Recall now from
\lx@sectionsign
B
that for any
n\geq 1
we have the
T
-equivariant Poisson isomorphism

J_{n}:\;(\Gamma_{n},\widetilde{\pi}_{n})\longrightarrow(F_{n}^{o}\times T,\,\pi_{n}\bowtie 0),\;J_{n}([g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}})=([g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}},\,[g_{1}g_{2}\cdots g_{n}]_{0}).

For
m,n\geq 1
, composing
J_{m+n}
with the piece-wise Poisson isomorphisms from
(G_{m,n},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})
to

(\Gamma_{m+n},\widetilde{\pi}_{m+n})
in Theorem
C.5
, we obtain piece-wise Poisson isomorphism from
(G_{m,n},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})
to

(F_{m+n}^{o}\times T,\pi_{m+n}\bowtie 0)
, which we state in the next Corollary
C.8
.

Corollary C.8
.

For any
m\geq 1
,
{\bf v}=(v_{1},\ldots,v_{n})\in W^{n}
, and

\dot{{\bf v}}=({\dot{v}}_{1},\ldots,{\dot{v}}_{n})\in{\bf v}T^{n}
, one has the
T
-equivariant Poisson isomorphism

J_{m+n}\circ\left(E_{m,\dot{{\bf v}}}|_{G_{m,n}^{{\bf v}}}\right):\;(G_{m,n}^{{\bf v}},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})\longrightarrow\left(F_{m+n}^{o}\cap\left(\widetilde{F}_{m}\times_{B}(B{\bf v}^{-1}B/B)\right)\times T,\,\pi_{m+n}\bowtie 0\right),

explicitly given by

\left([g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m}},\,[b_{-}\dot{c}_{1},\,\dot{c}_{2},\ldots,\,\dot{c}_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{-n}}\right)\longmapsto\left([g_{1},\ldots,g_{m},\,\dot{c}_{n}^{-1},\ldots,\dot{c}_{2}^{-1},\,\dot{c}_{1}^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{m+n}},\,[b_{-}]_{0}\right),

(C.7)

where
[g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m}}\in\widetilde{F}_{m},\,b_{-}\in B_{-}
, and
(\dot{c}_{1},\,\ldots,\,\dot{c}_{n})\in C_{\dot{{\bf v}}}
are such that

g_{1}\cdots g_{m}=b_{-}\dot{c}_{1}\cdots\dot{c}_{n}
.
In particular, for any
{\bf u}\in W^{m}
, one has the
T
-equivariant isomorphism

K_{{\bf u},\dot{{\bf v}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm def}}}{{=}}J_{m+n}\circ\left(E_{m,\dot{{\bf v}}}|_{G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}}\right):\;(G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})\longrightarrow\left({\mathcal{O}}^{({\bf u},{\bf v}^{-1})}_{e}\times T,\,\pi_{m+n}\bowtie 0\right)

(C.8)

of single
T
-leaves, explicitly given by (
C.7
) by restricting
[g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m}}
to
B{\bf u}B\subset\widetilde{F}_{m}
.

Note now that for any
n\geq 1
, the map

(\widetilde{F}_{n},\widetilde{\pi}_{n})\longrightarrow(G_{n,1},\widetilde{\pi}_{n,1}),\;[g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}}\longmapsto([g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}},\,g_{1}g_{2}\cdots g_{n}),

(C.9)

is a Poisson isomorphism due to the definition of the Poisson structure
\widetilde{\pi}_{n,1}
on
G_{n,1}
. Applying Corollary
C.8
to
G_{n,1}
, we
also obtain piece-wise
T
-equivariant Poisson isomorphisms from
(\widetilde{F}_{n},\widetilde{\pi}_{n})
to
(F_{n+1}^{o}\times T,\pi_{2n+1}\bowtie 0)
which carry

T
-leaves to
T
-leaves. To give the precise statement, recall from Proposition
3.1
that
T
-leaves of

(\widetilde{F}_{n},\widetilde{\pi}_{n})
are precisely of the form

\widetilde{F}_{n}^{{\bf u},v}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm def}}}{{=}}(B{\bf u}B)\cap\mu_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}}^{-1}(B_{-}vB_{-}),

(C.10)

where
{\bf u}\in W^{n}
and
v\in W
, and that

\mu_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}}:\;(\widetilde{F}_{n},\widetilde{\pi}_{n})\longrightarrow(G,\pi_{\rm st}),\;\;[g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}}\longmapsto g_{1}g_{2}\cdots g_{n}.

Corollary C.9
.

For any
v\in W
and
{\dot{v}}\in vT
, one has the
T
-equivariant Poisson isomorphism

J_{n,{\dot{v}}}:\;\mu_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}}^{-1}(B_{-}vB_{-})\longrightarrow\left(\left(F_{n+1}^{o}\cap\left(\widetilde{F}_{n}\times_{B}(Bv^{-1}B)/B\right)\right)\times T,\,\pi_{n+1}\bowtie 0\right)

explicitly given by

J_{n,{\dot{v}}}([g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}})=([g_{1},\ldots,g_{n},\,{\dot{v}}^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n+1}},\,[g_{1}\cdots g_{n}{\dot{v}}^{-1}]_{0}).

(C.11)

In particular, for any
{\bf u}\in W^{n}
, one has the
T
-equivariant isomorphism

J_{{\bf u},{\dot{v}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm def}}}{{=}}J_{n,{\dot{v}}}|_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}^{{\bf u},v}}:\;\widetilde{F}_{n}^{{\bf u},v}\longrightarrow({\mathcal{O}}^{({\bf u},v^{-1})}_{e}\times T,\,\pi_{n+1}\bowtie 0)

(C.12)

of single
T
-leaves.

Proof.

By Corollary
C.8
and using the isomorphism in (
C.9
), we have the Poisson isomorphism

J_{n,{\dot{v}}}:\;\mu_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}}^{-1}(B_{-}vB_{-})\longrightarrow\left(\left(F_{n+1}^{o}\cap\left(\widetilde{F}_{n}\times_{B}(Bv^{-1}B)/B\right)\right)\times T,\,\pi_{n+1}\bowtie 0\right)

given by

J_{n,{\dot{v}}}([g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}})=([g_{1},\ldots,g_{n},\,\dot{c}^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n+1}},\,[b_{-}]_{0})
,
where
[g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}}\in\mu_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}}^{-1}(B_{-}vB_{-})
, and we write
g_{1}g_{2}\cdots g_{n}=b_{-}\dot{c}
with unique
b_{-}\in B_{-}

and
\dot{c}\in C_{{\dot{v}}}
. Since
\dot{c}^{-1}\in{\dot{v}}^{-1}N
and
[b_{-}]_{0}=[g_{1}\cdots g_{n}{\dot{v}}^{-1}]_{0}
,
J_{n,{\dot{v}}}
is also given as in
(
C.11
).

Q.E.D.

C.3.
Proof of Theorem
C.5

We first prove two auxiliary lemmas.

Consider the quotient space

F_{n}^{\prime}=B\backslash\widetilde{F}_{n}=B\backslash\overbrace{G\times_{B}\cdots\times_{B}G}^{n},

where
B
acts on
\widetilde{F}_{n}
as a subgroup of
G
via the action of
G
on
\widetilde{F}_{n}
by (
3.3
), and denote by

\pi_{n}^{\prime}
the Poisson structure on
F_{n}^{\prime}
that is the quotient Poisson structure of
\widetilde{\pi}_{n}
on
\widetilde{F}_{n}
.
Define similarly the quotient
Poisson manifold
(F_{-n}^{\prime},\pi_{-n}^{\prime})
of
(\widetilde{F}_{-n},\widetilde{\pi}_{-n})
, where

F_{-n}^{\prime}=B_{-}\backslash\widetilde{F}_{-n}=B_{-}\backslash\overbrace{G\times_{B_{-}}\cdots\times_{B_{-}}G}^{n}.

For every

{\bf w}\in W^{n}
,
B\backslash B{\bf w}B
is then a Poisson sub-manifold of
(F_{n}^{\prime},\pi_{n}^{\prime})
and

B_{-}\backslash B_{-}{\bf w}B_{-}
a Poisson sub-manifold of
(F_{-n}^{\prime},\pi_{-n}^{\prime})
.

Fix now
{\bf v}=(v_{1},\ldots,v_{n})\in W^{n}
and any
\dot{{\bf v}}=({\dot{v}}_{1},\ldots,{\dot{v}}_{n})\in{\bf v}T^{n}
.
By (
4.32
) and (
4.25
), we have parametrizations

\displaystyle C_{\dot{{\bf v}}}\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\,B_{-}\backslash B_{-}{\bf v}B_{-}:\;\;(\dot{c}_{1},\dot{c}_{2},\ldots,\dot{c}_{n})\longmapsto[\dot{c}_{1},\,\dot{c}_{2},\,\ldots,\,\dot{c}_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{-n}^{\prime}},

\displaystyle C_{\dot{{\bf v}}}\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\,B\backslash B{\bf v}^{-1}B:\;\;(\dot{c}_{1},\dot{c}_{2},\ldots,\dot{c}_{n})\longmapsto[\dot{c}_{n}^{-1},\,\ldots,\,\dot{c}_{2}^{-1},\,\dot{c}_{1}^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}^{\prime}}.

One thus has the isomorphism

\psi_{\dot{{\bf v}}}:\;B_{-}\backslash B_{-}{\bf v}B_{-}\longrightarrow B\backslash B{\bf v}^{-1}B,\;\;[\dot{c}_{1},\,\dot{c}_{2},\,\ldots,\,\dot{c}_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{-n}^{\prime}}\longmapsto[\dot{c}_{n}^{-1},\,\ldots,\,\dot{c}_{2}^{-1},\,\dot{c}_{1}^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}^{\prime}},

where
(\dot{c}_{1},\,\dot{c}_{2},\,\ldots,\,\dot{c}_{n})\in C_{\dot{{\bf v}}}
.

Lemma C.10
.

The map

\psi_{\dot{{\bf v}}}:(B_{-}\backslash B_{-}{\bf v}B_{-},\,\pi_{-n}^{\prime})\to(B\backslash B{\bf v}^{-1}B,\,\pi_{n}^{\prime})

(C.13)

is a Poisson isomorphism.

Proof.

Define
I_{\dot{{\bf v}}}:B_{-}\backslash B_{-}{\bf v}B_{-}\to B{\bf v}B/B
by

I_{\dot{{\bf v}}}([\dot{c}_{1},\,\dot{c}_{2},\,\ldots,\,\dot{c}_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{-n}^{\prime}})=[\dot{c}_{1},\,\dot{c}_{2},\,\ldots,\,\dot{c}_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}},\hskip 14.45377pt(\dot{c}_{1},\,\dot{c}_{2},\,\ldots,\,\dot{c}_{n})\in C_{\dot{{\bf v}}}.

By
[
24
, Lemma 7.1]
,
I_{\dot{{\bf v}}}:(B_{-}\backslash B_{-}{\bf v}B_{-},\,\pi_{-n}^{\prime})\to(B{\bf v}B/B,\,-\pi_{n})
is Poisson.
One also has the Poisson isomorphism

I_{n}:\;(F_{n},\pi_{n})\longrightarrow(F_{n}^{\prime},-\pi_{n}^{\prime}),\;[g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}}\longmapsto[g_{n}^{-1},\ldots,g_{2}^{-1},g_{1}^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}^{\prime}}.

(C.14)

Thus
\phi_{\dot{{\bf v}}}=I_{n}\circ I_{\dot{{\bf v}}}
is Poisson as stated.

Q.E.D.

Recall from Example
A.4
the pair
((B_{-}^{\rm op},\pi_{\rm st}),(B,\pi_{\rm st}))
of dual Poisson Lie groups. Note that one has the left Poisson action

\displaystyle\lambda_{{\bf v}}^{-}:

\displaystyle\;\;(B_{-}^{\rm op},\pi_{\rm st})\times(B_{-}\backslash B_{-}{\bf v}B_{-},\,\pi_{-n}^{\prime})\longrightarrow(B_{-}\backslash B_{-}{\bf v}B_{-},\,\pi_{-n}^{\prime}),

(C.15)

\displaystyle\;\;(b_{-},\;[g_{1},\,\ldots,\,g_{n-1},\,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{-n}^{\prime}})\longmapsto[g_{1},\,\ldots,\,g_{n-1},\,g_{n}b_{-}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{-n}^{\prime}},

(C.16)

of the Poisson Lie group
(B_{-}^{\rm op},\pi_{\rm st})
. Let

\lambda_{\dot{{\bf v}}^{-1}}:\;(B_{-}^{\rm op},\pi_{\rm st})\times(B\backslash B{\bf v}^{-1}B,\,\pi_{n}^{\prime})\longrightarrow(B\backslash B{\bf v}^{-1}B,\,\pi_{n}^{\prime})

(C.17)

be the unique left Poisson action of
(B_{-}^{\rm op},\pi_{\rm st})
on
(B\backslash B{\bf v}^{-1}B,\,\pi_{n}^{\prime})
such that

\psi_{\dot{{\bf v}}}
in (
C.13
)
becomes an isomorphism of Poisson manifolds with left Poisson actions by the Poisson Lie group
(B_{-}^{\rm op},\pi_{\rm st})
.
Let
\rho_{+}
be the right Poisson action of
(B,\pi_{\rm st})
on itself by right translation. Using the pair
(\rho_{+},\lambda_{\dot{{\bf v}}^{-1}})
of respectively right and left Poisson
actions of
the Poisson Lie groups
(B,\pi_{\rm st})
and
(B_{-}^{\rm op},\pi_{\rm st})
, one then has the mixed product Poisson structure

\pi_{\rm st}\times_{(\rho_{+},\lambda_{\dot{{\bf v}}^{-1}})}\pi_{n}^{\prime}
on
B\times(B\backslash B{\bf v}^{-1}B)
. Define the isomorphism

\displaystyle K_{\dot{{\bf v}}}:

\displaystyle\;\;B{\bf v}^{-1}B\longrightarrow B\times(B\backslash B{\bf v}^{-1}B),\;

\displaystyle\;\;[b\dot{c}_{n}^{-1},\dot{c}_{n-1}^{-1},\ldots,\dot{c}_{1}^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}}\longmapsto(b,\,[\dot{c}_{n}^{-1},\dot{c}_{n-1}^{-1},\ldots,\dot{c}_{1}^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}^{\prime}}),

where again
(\dot{c}_{1},\ldots,\dot{c}_{n-1},\dot{c}_{n})\in C_{\dot{{\bf v}}}
.

Lemma C.11
.

The map
K_{\dot{{\bf v}}}:(B{\bf v}^{-1}B,\,\widetilde{\pi}_{n})\to(B\times(B\backslash B{\bf v}^{-1}B),\,\pi_{\rm st}\times_{(\rho_{+},\lambda_{\dot{{\bf v}}^{-1}})}\pi_{n}^{\prime})

is a Poisson isomorphism.

Proof.

Note first that
K_{\dot{{\bf v}}}=K_{n}\circ J_{\dot{{\bf v}}}\circ\tilde{I}_{n}
with

B{\bf v}^{-1}B\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tilde{I}_{n}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\,B{\bf v}B\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle J_{\dot{{\bf v}}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\,(B{\bf v}B/B)\times B\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle K_{n}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\,B\times(B\backslash B{\bf v}^{-1}B),

where
\tilde{I}_{n}:B{\bf v}^{-1}B\to B{\bf v}B,[g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}}\to[g_{n}^{-1},\ldots,g_{2}^{-1},g_{1}^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}}
,

J_{\dot{{\bf v}}}:\;B{\bf v}B\longrightarrow(B{\bf v}B/B)\times B,\;[\dot{c}_{1},\ldots,\dot{c}_{n-1},\dot{c}_{n}b]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}}\longmapsto([\dot{c}_{1},\ldots,\dot{c}_{n-1},\dot{c}_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}},\,b),

where
(\dot{c}_{1},\ldots,\dot{c}_{n-1},\dot{c}_{n})\in C_{\dot{{\bf v}}}
, and

K_{n}:(B{\bf v}B/B)\times B\to B\times(B\backslash B{\bf v}^{-1}B)
is given by

K_{n}([\dot{c}_{1},\ldots,\dot{c}_{n-1},\dot{c}_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}},\,b)\longmapsto(b^{-1},\;[\dot{c}_{n}^{-1},\,\dot{c}_{n-1}^{-1},\,\ldots,\,\dot{c}_{1}^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}^{\prime}}),

where again
(\dot{c}_{1},\ldots,\dot{c}_{n-1},\dot{c}_{n})\in C_{\dot{{\bf v}}}
. It is clear that
\tilde{I}_{n}:(B{\bf v}^{-1}B,\,\widetilde{\pi}_{n})\to(B{\bf v}B,\,-\widetilde{\pi}_{n})
is Poisson.
Denote
\pi=\pi_{\rm st}\times_{(\rho_{+},\lambda_{\dot{{\bf v}}^{-1}})}\pi_{n}^{\prime}
. To show
K_{\dot{{\bf v}}}(\widetilde{\pi}_{n})=\pi
, one needs to show

J_{\dot{{\bf v}}}(\widetilde{\pi}_{n})=-K_{n}^{-1}(\pi)

as Poisson structures on
(B{\bf v}B/B)\times B
. The Poisson structure
J_{\dot{{\bf v}}}(\widetilde{\pi}_{n})
has been shown in

[
24
, Proposition 7.3]
to be a mixed product. We now compute
-K_{n}^{-1}(\pi)
using the definition of
\pi
and then compare

-K_{n}^{-1}(\pi)
with the formula for
J_{\dot{{\bf v}}}(\widetilde{\pi}_{n})
given in
[
24
, Proposition 7.3]
.

Let
\{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\dim\mathfrak{b}_{-}}
be any basis of
\mathfrak{b}_{-}
with
\{x^{i}\}_{i=1}^{\dim\mathfrak{b}_{-}}
the dual basis of
\mathfrak{b}
under the pairing between

\langle\,,\,\rangle_{(\mathfrak{b}_{-},\mathfrak{b})}
in (
A.3
). Recall that for
x\in\mathfrak{b}
,
x^{L}
(resp.
x^{R}
)
denotes the left (resp. right) invariant vector field of
B
with value
x
at the
identity element of
B
. By the definition of
\pi
, we have

\displaystyle-K_{n}^{-1}(\pi)

\displaystyle=K_{n}^{-1}\left((-\pi_{\rm st},\,-\pi_{n}^{\prime})+\sum_{i=1}^{\dim\mathfrak{b}_{-}}((x^{i})^{L},\,0)\wedge(0,\,\lambda_{\dot{{\bf v}}^{-1}}(x_{i}))\right)

\displaystyle=(\pi_{n},\,\pi_{\rm st})+\sum_{i=1}^{\dim\mathfrak{b}_{-}}(I_{n}^{-1}(\lambda_{\dot{{\bf v}}^{-1}}(x_{i})),\,0)\wedge(0,\,(x^{i})^{R}),

(C.18)

where
I_{n}:F_{n}\to F_{n}^{\prime}
is given in (
C.14
).

Consider now the Poisson Lie groups
(B_{-},-\pi_{\rm st})
and
(B,\pi_{\rm st})
which become dual Poisson
Lie groups under the pairing
-\langle\,,\,\rangle_{(\mathfrak{b}_{-},\mathfrak{b})}
. Let now the right Poisson action

\displaystyle\;\;(B_{-}\backslash B_{-}{\bf v}B_{-},\,-\pi_{-n}^{\prime})\times(B_{-},-\pi_{\rm st})\longrightarrow(B_{-}\backslash B_{-}{\bf v}B_{-},\,-\pi_{-n}^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\;\;([g_{1},\,\ldots,\,g_{n-1},\,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{-n}^{\prime}},\,b_{-})\longmapsto[g_{1},\,\ldots,\,g_{n-1},\,g_{n}b_{-}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{-n}^{\prime}},

of the Poisson Lie group
(B_{-},-\pi_{\rm st})
, and let

\rho_{\dot{{\bf v}}}:\;(B{\bf v}B/B,\,\pi_{n})\times(B_{-},-\pi_{\rm st})\longrightarrow(B{\bf v}B/B,\,\pi_{n})

be the unique right Poisson action of
(B_{-},-\pi_{\rm st})
on
(B{\bf v}B/B,\,\pi_{n})
such that

I_{\dot{{\bf v}}}:\;\;(B_{-}\backslash B_{-}{\bf v}B_{-},\,-\pi_{-n}^{\prime})\longrightarrow(B{\bf v}^{-1}B/B,\,\pi_{n})

becomes an isomorphism of Poisson manifolds with right Poisson actions by the Poisson Lie group
(B_{-},-\pi_{\rm st})
.
Let
\lambda_{+}
be the left Poisson action of
(B,\pi_{\rm st})
on itself by left translation. Using the pair
(\rho_{\dot{{\bf v}}},\lambda_{+})
of
respectively right and left Poisson actions
of
the Poisson Lie groups
(B_{-},-\pi_{\rm st})
and
(B,\pi_{\rm st})
, one then has the mixed product Poisson structure

\pi_{n}\times_{(\rho_{\dot{{\bf v}}},\lambda_{+})}\pi_{\rm st}
on
(B{\bf v}B/B)\times B
. By
[
24
, Proposition 7.3]
,

J_{\dot{{\bf v}}}(\widetilde{\pi}_{n})=\pi_{n}\times_{(\rho_{\dot{{\bf v}}},\lambda_{+})}\pi_{\rm st}=(\pi_{n},\,\pi_{\rm st})+\sum_{i=1}^{\dim\mathfrak{b}_{-}}(\rho_{\dot{{\bf v}}}(x_{i}),\,0)\wedge(0,\,(x^{i})^{R}).

One now checks from the definitions of the actions
\rho_{\dot{{\bf v}}}
and
\lambda_{\dot{{\bf v}}^{-1}}
that
