\rho_{\dot{{\bf v}}}(x)=I_{n}^{-1}(\lambda_{\dot{{\bf v}}^{-1}}(x))\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{1}(B{\bf v}B/B),\hskip 14.45377pt\forall\;x\in\mathfrak{b}_{-}.

Comparing with (
C.18
), one shows (
C.18
). This finishes the proof of Lemma
C.11
.

Q.E.D.

Remark C.12
.

The proof of
[
24
, Proposition 7.3]
uses a quotient space of the Drinfeld double of
(G,\pi_{\rm st})
, and one can
also use similar arguments to prove Lemma
C.11
directly.

\diamond

Proof of Theorem
C.5
.
Let
p_{-n}^{\prime}:\widetilde{F}_{-n}\to F_{-n}^{\prime}
be the projection map, and let

P_{n}={\rm Id}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m}}\times p_{-n}^{\prime}:\;\;\widetilde{F}_{m}\times B_{-}{\bf v}B_{-}\longrightarrow\widetilde{F}_{m}\times(B_{-}\backslash B_{-}{\bf v}B_{-}).

By the definition of the Poisson structure
\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n}
,

P_{n}:\;(\widetilde{F}_{m}\times B_{-}{\bf v}B_{-},\;\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})\longrightarrow(\widetilde{F}_{m}\times(B_{-}\backslash B_{-}{\bf v}B_{-}),\;\widetilde{\pi}_{m}\times_{(\tilde{\rho}_{m},\lambda_{{\bf v}}^{-})}\pi_{-n}^{\prime})

is Poisson, where
\lambda_{{\bf v}}^{-}
is given in (
C.15
), and
\tilde{\rho}_{m}
is the right Poisson action

\tilde{\rho}_{m}:\;(\widetilde{F}_{m},\,\widetilde{\pi}_{m})\times(B,\pi_{\rm st})\longrightarrow(\widetilde{F}_{m},\,\widetilde{\pi}_{m}),\;([g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{m}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m}},b)\longmapsto[g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{m}b]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m}}.

Let
P_{n}^{\prime}={\rm Id}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m}}\times\phi_{\dot{{\bf v}}}:\widetilde{F}_{m}\times(B_{-}\backslash B_{-}{\bf v}B_{-})\to\widetilde{F}_{m}\times(B{\bf v}^{-1}B/B)
.
By Lemma
C.10
and by the definition of the Poisson action

\lambda_{\dot{{\bf v}}^{-1}}
in (
C.17
),

P_{n}^{\prime}:\;(\widetilde{F}_{m}\times(B_{-}\backslash B_{-}{\bf v}B_{-}),\;\widetilde{\pi}_{m}\times_{(\tilde{\rho}_{m},\lambda_{{\bf v}}^{-})}\pi_{-n}^{\prime})\longrightarrow(\widetilde{F}_{m}\times(B{\bf v}^{-1}B/B),\,\widetilde{\pi}_{m}\times_{(\tilde{\rho}_{m},\lambda_{\dot{{\bf v}}^{-1}})}\pi_{n})

is Poisson. On the other hand, by Lemma
C.11
and Example
A.3
, the map

\displaystyle Q_{n}:

\displaystyle\;(\widetilde{F}_{m}\times(B{\bf v}^{-1}B/B),\,\widetilde{\pi}_{m}\times_{(\tilde{\rho}_{m},\lambda_{\dot{{\bf v}}^{-1}})}\pi_{n})\longrightarrow(\widetilde{F}_{m}\times_{B}B{\bf v}^{-1}B,\,\widetilde{\pi}_{m+n}),

\displaystyle\;\;([g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{m}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m}},\;[\dot{c}_{n}^{-1},\,\ldots,\,\dot{c}_{2}^{-1},\,\dot{c}_{1}^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}})\longmapsto[g_{1},\,g_{2},\,\ldots,\,g_{m},\,\dot{c}_{n}^{-1},\,\ldots,\,\dot{c}_{2}^{-1},\,\dot{c}_{1}^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m+n}},

is a Poisson isomorphism, where
g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}\in G
and
(\dot{c}_{1},\dot{c}_{2},\ldots,\dot{c}_{n})\in C_{\dot{{\bf v}}}
. As

E_{m,\dot{{\bf v}}}=Q_{n}\circ P_{n}^{\prime}\circ P_{n},

one concludes that
E_{m,\dot{{\bf v}}}
is Poisson as stated. This finishes the proof of Theorem
C.5
.

Appendix D
Symplectic leaves of
({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T,\pi_{n}\bowtie 0)

In this appendix, we assume that
G
is connected and simply connected, and we describe in Theorem
D.12

the symplectic leaves of

({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T,\pi_{n}\bowtie 0)
for arbitrary
{\bf w}\in W^{n}
. We then apply Theorem
D.12

to obtain explicit descriptions of all the symplectic
leaves in the three series

(F_{n}^{o}\times T,\,\pi_{n}\bowtie 0),\hskip 14.45377pt(G_{m+n},\,\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n}),\hskip 14.45377pt(\widetilde{F}_{n},\,\widetilde{\pi}_{n}),\hskip 14.45377ptm,n\geq 1.

In particular, we describe all symplectic leaves in all generalized double Bruhat cells
G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}
,
generalizing the result of
[
13
]
for the case
of
u,v\in W
.

D.1.
Notation

Assume that
G
is connected and simply connected. Recall from
\lx@sectionsign
3.1
that
\mathfrak{h}
denotes the Lie algebra of
the maximal torus
T=B\cap B_{-}
of
G
.
Let
X_{*}(T)
and
X^{*}(T)
be respectively the co-character and the character lattices of
T
.
Then

X_{*}(T)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbb{C}}=\mathfrak{h}\hskip 14.45377pt\mbox{and}\hskip 14.45377ptX^{*}(T)\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\mathbb{C}}=\mathfrak{h}^{*},

and we also regard
X_{*}(T)
and
X^{*}(T)
as respective subsets of
\mathfrak{h}
and
\mathfrak{h}^{*}
.
For
\lambda\in X^{*}(T)
and
t\in T
, write
t^{\lambda}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
for the value of
\lambda
on
t
.
Recall that for
w\in W
, we
denote by
wT
the set of all representative of
w
in the normalizer subgroup
N_{\scriptscriptstyle G}(T)
of
T
.
One has the right action

T\times W\longrightarrow T,\;\;(t,w)\longmapsto t^{w}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm def}}}{{=}}\dot{w}^{-1}t\dot{w},

where
\dot{w}\in wT
for
w\in W
, and
(t^{w})^{\lambda}=t^{w\lambda}
for
t\in T,w\in W
, and
\lambda\in X^{*}(T)
.

Let
\Phi_{0}\subset X^{*}(T)
and
\{\alpha^{\vee}:\alpha\in\Phi_{0}\}\subset X_{*}(T)
be respectively the set of simple roots and the set of simple
co-roots determined by
B
.
For
\alpha\in\Phi_{0}
, fix root vectors
e_{\alpha}
for
\alpha
and

e_{-\alpha}
for
-\alpha
such that
[e_{\alpha},e_{-\alpha}]=\alpha^{\vee}\in\mathfrak{h}
.
Let

x_{\pm\alpha}:{\mathbb{C}}\to G
be the one-parameter subgroups given by

x_{\alpha}(z)=\exp(z\,e_{\alpha}),\hskip 14.45377ptx_{-\alpha}(z)=\exp(z\,e_{-\alpha}),\hskip 14.45377ptz\in{\mathbb{C}}.

For
\alpha\in\Phi_{0}
, let
s_{\alpha}\in W
be the corresponding simple reflection,
and choose
\overline{s}_{\alpha}\in s_{\alpha}T

by
\overline{s}_{\alpha}=x_{\alpha}(-1)x_{-\alpha}(1)x_{\alpha}(-1)
.
For future use, we also note that

x_{-\alpha}(z)=x_{\alpha}(z^{-1})\overline{s}_{\alpha}\alpha^{\vee}(z)x_{\alpha}(z^{-1}),\hskip 14.45377pt\alpha\in\Phi_{0},\;z\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}.

(D.1)

By
[
8
,
\lx@sectionsign
1.4]
, for any reduced decomposition
w=s_{\alpha_{1}}s_{\alpha_{2}}\cdots s_{\alpha_{l}}
of
w
, the element

\overline{w}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}\overline{s}_{\alpha_{1}}\;\overline{s}_{\alpha_{2}}\;\cdots\;\overline{s}_{\alpha_{l}}\in N_{\scriptscriptstyle G}(T)

(D.2)

represents
w
and is independent of the choice of the reduced decomposition.

Let
\{\omega_{\alpha}:\alpha\in\Phi_{0}\}
be the set of fundamental weights. For
\alpha\in\Phi_{0}
, let
\Delta^{\omega_{\alpha}}\in{\mathbb{C}}[G]

be the corresponding principal minor on
G
, uniquely determined by

\Delta^{\omega_{\alpha}}(g_{-}g_{0}g_{+})=(g_{0})^{\omega_{\alpha}},\hskip 14.45377ptg_{-}\in N_{-},\,g_{0}\in T,\,g_{+}\in N.

By
[
8
, Proposition 2.3]
, when
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\in\Phi_{0}
and
\alpha\neq\alpha^{\prime}
, one has

\Delta^{\omega_{\alpha}}(gx_{\alpha^{\prime}}(z)\overline{s}_{\alpha^{\prime}})=\Delta^{\omega_{\alpha}}(g),\hskip 14.45377pt\forall\;\;g\in G,\,z\in{\mathbb{C}}.

(D.3)

More generally, let
u\in W
, let
{\rm supp}(u)
be the set of all
\alpha\in\Phi_{0}
such that
s_{\alpha}
appears in some, equivalently every,
reduced decomposition of
u
, and let

{\rm supp}^{o}(u)=\{\alpha\in\Phi_{0}:u\omega_{\alpha}=\omega_{\alpha}\}.

Recall that
C_{\overline{u}}=N\overline{u}\cap\overline{u}N_{-}
.
If
u=s_{\alpha_{1}}s_{\alpha_{2}}\cdots s_{\alpha_{l}}
is a reduced decomposition of
u
, setting
{\bf u}=(s_{\alpha_{1}},\ldots,s_{\alpha_{l}})
,
one then has the isomorphism
[
8
, Proposition 2.11]

g_{{\bf u}}:\;\;{\mathbb{C}}^{l}\longrightarrow C_{\overline{u}},\;g_{\bf u}(z_{1},z_{2},\ldots,z_{l})=x_{\alpha_{1}}(z_{1})\overline{s}_{\alpha_{1}}\,x_{\alpha_{2}}(z_{2})\overline{s}_{\alpha_{2}}\,\cdots\,x_{\alpha_{l}}(z_{l})\overline{s}_{\alpha_{l}}.

(D.4)

It then follows from (
D.3
) that

\Delta^{\omega_{\alpha}}(gc)=\Delta^{\omega_{\alpha}}(cg)=\Delta^{\omega_{\alpha}}(g),\hskip 14.45377pt\forall\;\;\alpha\in{\rm supp}^{o}(u),\;g\in G,\,\;c\in C_{\overline{u}}.

(D.5)

We also note the open embedding
[
21
, Proposition 2.7]
(see also
[
8
, Proposition 2.18]
)

m_{\bf u}:\;\ ({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})^{l}\longrightarrow N_{-}\cap BuB,\;\;m_{\bf u}(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\ldots,\varepsilon_{l})=x_{-\alpha_{1}}(\varepsilon_{1})x_{-\alpha_{2}}(\varepsilon_{2})\cdots x_{-\alpha_{l}}(\varepsilon_{l}).

(D.6)

D.2.
Description of symplectic leaves of
({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T,\pi_{n}\bowtie 0)

Let
{\bf w}=(w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})\in W^{n}
, and let
\dot{{\bf w}}=(\dot{w}_{1},\ldots,\dot{w}_{n})\in{\bf w}T^{n}
be arbitrary.
Recall from (
4.22
) that

C_{\dot{{\bf w}}}=C_{\dot{w}_{1}}\times\cdots\times C_{\dot{w}_{n}}
.
By (
4.24
), one has the isomorphism

\rho_{{\dot{{\bf w}}}}:\;\;C_{{\dot{{\bf w}}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w},\;\;(\dot{c}_{1},\dot{c}_{2},\ldots,\dot{c}_{n})\longmapsto[\dot{c}_{1},\dot{c}_{2},\ldots,\dot{c}_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}}.

(D.7)

Recall that
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}=\{[g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}}\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}:g_{1}g_{2}\cdots g_{n}\in B_{-}B\}
. For

(\dot{c}_{1},\dot{c}_{2},\ldots,\dot{c}_{n})\in C_{{\dot{{\bf w}}}}
, then

[\dot{c}_{1},\dot{c}_{2},\ldots,\dot{c}_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}}\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\hskip 14.45377pt\mbox{iff}\hskip 14.45377pt\dot{c}_{1}\dot{c}_{2}\cdots\dot{c}_{n}\in B_{-}B.

Recall also that for
x\in B_{-}B
, we write
x=[x]_{-}[x]_{0}[x]_{+}
, where
[x]_{-}\in N_{-},[x]_{0}\in T
, and
[x]_{+}\in N
.
One thus has the well-defined map

\tau_{\dot{{\bf w}}}:\;{\mathcal{O}}^{{\bf w}}_{e}\longrightarrow T,\;[\dot{c}_{1},\dot{c}_{2},\ldots,\dot{c}_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}}\longmapsto[\dot{c}_{1}\dot{c}_{2}\cdots\dot{c}_{n}]_{0},

(D.8)

where
(\dot{c}_{1},\dot{c}_{2},\ldots,\dot{c}_{n})\in C_{{\dot{{\bf w}}}}
and
\dot{c}_{1}\dot{c}_{2}\cdots\dot{c}_{n}\in B_{-}B
.
Recall the
T
-action on
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}\subset F_{n}
in (
1.2
).

Lemma D.1
.

For any
{\bf w}=(w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})\in W^{n}
and
\dot{{\bf w}}=(\dot{w}_{1},\ldots,\dot{w}_{n})\in{\bf w}T^{n}
,
one has

\tau_{\dot{{\bf w}}}(a\cdot q)=h(h^{-1})^{w}\tau_{\dot{{\bf w}}}(q),\hskip 14.45377pth\in T,\,q\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e},

(D.9)

where
w=w_{1}w_{2}\cdots w_{n}\in W
.

Proof.

Let
(\dot{c}_{1},\ldots,\dot{c}_{n})\in C_{\overline{{\bf w}}}
and write
\dot{c}_{i}=x_{i}\dot{w}_{i}
, where

x_{i}\in N\cap\dot{w}_{i}N_{-}\dot{w}_{i}^{\,-1}
for
i\in[1,n]
. For
h\in T
, one then has

h\cdot[\dot{c}_{1},\dot{c}_{2},\ldots,\dot{c}_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}}=[h\dot{c}_{1},\dot{c}_{2},\ldots,\dot{c}_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}}=[x_{1}^{\prime}\dot{w}_{1},x_{2}^{\prime}\dot{w}_{2},\ldots,x_{n}^{\prime}\dot{w}_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}},

where
x_{1}^{\prime}=hx_{1}h^{-1}
and
x_{i}^{\prime}=h^{w_{1}\cdots w_{i-1}}x_{i}(h^{-1})^{w_{1}\cdots w_{i-1}}
for
i\in[2,n]
.
Now (
D.9
) follows from

(x_{1}^{\prime}\dot{w}_{1})(x_{2}^{\prime}\dot{w}_{2})\cdots(x_{n}^{\prime}\dot{w}_{n})=h\dot{c}_{1}\dot{c}_{2}\cdots\dot{c}_{n}(h^{-1})^{w}.

Q.E.D.

Remark D.2
.

The definition of the map
\tau_{\dot{{\bf w}}}:{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\to T
depends on the choice of
\dot{{\bf w}}\in{\bf w}T^{n}
as indicated in the notation.
If
\hat{{\bf w}}=(\hat{w}_{1},\ldots,\hat{w}_{n})\in{\bf w}T^{n}
is another choice, and if

\dot{w}_{1}\dot{w}_{2}\cdots\dot{w}_{n}=\hat{w}_{1}\hat{w}_{2}\cdots\hat{w}_{n}t\in wT^{n},

where
t\in T
,
then each
(\dot{c}_{1},\ldots,\dot{c}_{n})\in C_{\dot{{\bf w}}}
corresponds to a unique
(\hat{c}_{1},\ldots,\hat{c}_{n})\in C_{\hat{{\bf w}}}

such that

[\dot{c}_{1},\,\ldots,\,\dot{c}_{n-1},\,\dot{c}_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}}=[\hat{c}_{1},\,\ldots,\,\hat{c}_{n-1},\,\hat{c}_{n}t^{\prime}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}}
.
It follows that

\tau_{\dot{{\bf w}}}(q)=t\tau_{\hat{{\bf w}}}(q),\hskip 14.45377ptq\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}.

(D.10)

\diamond

Let again
{\bf w}=(w_{1},w_{2},\ldots,w_{n})\in W^{n}
and
w=w_{1}w_{2}\cdots w_{n}\in W
. Set

T^{w}=\{a(a^{-1})^{w}:a\in T\}.

(D.11)

Then
\dim T^{w}={\rm dim}({\rm Im}(1-w))
,
where
1-w:\mathfrak{h}\to\mathfrak{h}
. For

\dot{{\bf w}}\in{\bf w}T^{n}
, let

\displaystyle\mu_{\dot{{\bf w}}}:\;\;{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T\longrightarrow T/T^{w},\;\;(q,t)\longmapsto t^{-2}\tau_{\dot{{\bf w}}}(q)_{\cdot}T^{w}.

(D.12)

Recall that
l({\bf w})=l(w_{1})+l(w_{2})+\cdots+l(w_{n})
.

Proposition D.3
.

For any
{\bf w}\in W^{n}
and
\dot{{\bf w}}\in{\bf w}T^{n}
, symplectic leaves of
({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T,\pi_{n}\bowtie 0)
are precisely all
the connected components of the level sets of the map
\mu_{\dot{{\bf w}}}
.
In particular, all symplectic leaves of
({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T,\pi_{n}\bowtie 0)
have dimension

l({\bf w})+{\rm dim}{(\rm Im}(1-w))
.

Proof.

As

\mu_{{\dot{{\bf w}}}}
is a surjective submersion, all of its level sets are smooth and have dimension equal to

l({\bf w})+{\rm dim}{(\rm Im}(1-w))
.
By Remark
D.2
, the collection of level sets of the map
\mu_{\dot{{\bf w}}}
is
independent of the choice of the representative
\dot{{\bf w}}\in{\bf w}T^{n}
.
We may thus choose
\overline{{\bf w}}=(\overline{w}_{1},\ldots,\overline{w}_{n})\in{\bf w}T^{n}
.

For notational simplicity, we set
\pi=\pi_{n}\bowtie 0
, and let

\mu:\;{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T\longrightarrow T,\;\mu(q,t)=t^{-2}\tau_{\overline{{\bf w}}}(q).

For
(q,t)\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T
, let

\pi_{(q,t)}^{\#}:\;\;T^{*}_{(q,t)}({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T)\longrightarrow T_{(q,t)}({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T),\;\left(\pi_{(q,t)}^{\#}(\beta_{1}),\beta_{2}\right)=\pi(q,t)(\beta_{1},\beta_{2}),

where
\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\in T^{*}_{(q,t)}({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T)
,
and we use translation in
T
to identify the tangent space of
T
at
\mu(q,t)\in T
with
\mathfrak{h}={\rm Lie}(T)
.
Note that the Lie algebra of
T^{w}
is
{\rm Im}(1-w)\subset\mathfrak{h}
. As
\mu
is a surjective submersion,
it is enough to show that for every
(q,t)\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T
,

{\rm Im}\left(\pi_{(q,t)}^{\#}\right)=\mu_{*}^{-1}({\rm Im}(1-w)),

(D.13)

where
\mu_{*}:T_{(q,t)}({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T)\to T_{\mu(q,t)}T\cong\mathfrak{h}
is the differential of
\mu
at
(q,t)
.
Recall that
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T
is a single
T
-leaf of
\pi
, and that
by
[
16
, Proposition 2.24]
, the co-rank of
\pi
in
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T

is equal to the co-dimension of
T^{w}
in
T
. Thus the two vector spaces on the two sides of (
D.13
) have the same dimension, and it is
enough to show that

{\rm Im}\left(\pi_{(q,t)}^{\#}\right)\subset\mu_{*}^{-1}({\rm Im}(1-w))
.

For
\chi\in X^{*}(T)
, denote by
\mu^{\chi}
the regular function on
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T
defined by

\mu^{\chi}(q,t)=(\mu(q,t))^{\chi}=t^{-2\chi}\tau_{\overline{{\bf w}}}(q)^{\chi}.

For any regular function
f
on
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T
that is a weight
vector with weight
\chi_{f}\in X^{*}(T)
for the diagonal action of
T
on
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T
, one has
by
[
16
, Corollary 2.15]
and the definition
\pi
that

\{\mu^{\chi},\,f\}_{\pi}=\langle\chi-w\chi,\,\chi_{f}\rangle\mu^{\chi}f,

(D.14)

where
\{\,,\,\}_{\pi}
is the Poisson bracket on the coordinate ring of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T
defined by
\pi
. For
\chi\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}
, let
\tilde{\chi}

be the left invariant
1
-form on
T
with value
\chi
at the identity element. By (
D.14
),

\pi^{\#}(\mu^{*}(\tilde{\chi}))=\sigma(\chi^{\#}-w\chi^{\#})\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{1}({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T),

where
\sigma:\mathfrak{h}\to{\mathfrak{X}}^{1}({\mathcal{O}}^{w}_{e}\times T)
is the Lie algebra homomorphism defined by the diagonal
T
-action on

{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T
, and
\chi^{\#}\in\mathfrak{h}
is such that
\chi^{\prime}(\chi^{\#})=\langle\chi,\chi^{\prime}\rangle
for
\chi^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}
.
Note that for
\chi\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}
,
\chi|_{{\rm Im}(1-w)}=0
if and only if
\chi=w\chi
.
Now for
\chi\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}
with
\chi=w\chi
and
\beta\in T^{*}_{(q,t)}({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}\times T)
,

\left(\mu_{*}\left(\pi^{\#}_{(q,t)}(\beta),\,\chi\right)\right)=-\left(\beta,\;\pi^{\#}_{(q,t)}(\mu^{*}(\tilde{\chi}))\right)=-(\beta,\,\sigma(\chi^{\#}-w\chi^{\#}))=0.

This shows that
{\rm Im}\left(\pi_{(q,t)}^{\#}\right)\subset\mu_{*}^{-1}({\rm Im}(1-w))
and thus (
D.13
).

Q.E.D.

For
\dot{{\bf w}}=(\dot{w}_{1},\dot{w}_{2},\ldots,\dot{w}_{n})\in{\bf w}T^{n}
and for
a\in T
, we thus need to determine the connected components of the level set

\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{{\bf w}}}_{a}\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}\,\mu_{\dot{{\bf w}}}^{-1}(a_{\cdot}T^{w})=\{(q,t)\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T:\,t^{-2}\tau_{\dot{{\bf w}}}(q)\in aT^{w}\}.

(D.15)

To this end, let
{\rm supp}({\bf w})=\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}{\rm supp}(w_{i})
, and let

{\rm supp}^{o}({\bf w})=\Phi_{0}\backslash{\rm supp}({\bf w})=\bigcap_{i=1}^{n}{\rm supp}^{o}(w_{i})=\{\alpha\in\Phi_{0}:w_{i}\omega_{\alpha}=\omega_{\alpha},\,\forall\,i\in[1,n]\}.

Introduce the sub-torus

{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}=\{t\in T:t^{\omega_{\alpha}}=1,\,\forall\alpha\in{\rm supp}^{o}({\bf w})\}

(D.16)

of
T
. Note that
T^{w}\subset{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
and
that
\dim({\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}})=|{\rm supp}({\bf w})|
. Let

\delta_{\bf w}:\;{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T\longrightarrow T/{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}:\;(q,t)\longmapsto t_{\cdot}{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}},

(D.17)

and note that
\delta_{\bf w}
has the
T
-equivariance

\delta_{\bf w}(h\cdot(q,t))=h\delta_{\bf w}(q,t),\hskip 14.45377pth\in T,\,(q,t)\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T.

(D.18)

Let
t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}\in T
be such that

\dot{w}_{1}\dot{w}_{2}\cdots\dot{w}_{n}=\overline{w}_{1}\overline{w}_{2}\cdots\overline{w}_{n}\,t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}\in wT
.

Lemma D.4
.

For any
\dot{{\bf w}}=(\dot{w}_{1},\dot{w}_{2},\ldots,\dot{w}_{n})\in{\bf w}T^{n}
and
a\in T
, the restriction of the map

\delta_{\bf w}^{2}:\;{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T\longrightarrow T/{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}},\;(q,t)\longmapsto{t^{2}}_{\cdot}{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}},

to
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{{\bf w}}}_{a}\subset{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T
is a constant map:
one has
t^{2}\in a^{-1}t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
for all
(q,t)\in\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{{\bf w}}}_{a}
.

Proof.

By (
D.5
),
\tau_{\overline{{\bf w}}}(q)\in{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
for all
q\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}
, so by Remark
D.2
,

\tau_{\dot{{\bf w}}}(q)=t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}\,\tau_{\overline{{\bf w}}}(q)\in t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}\,{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}},\hskip 14.45377pt\forall\;q\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}.

(D.19)

It follows that for every
(q,t)\in\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{{\bf w}}}_{a}
, one has

t^{-2}\in at_{\dot{{\bf w}}}^{-1}{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
, and thus

t^{2}\in a^{-1}t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}\,{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
.

Q.E.D.

Remark D.5
.

By (
D.5
),
\Delta^{\omega_{\alpha}}(\overline{w}_{1}\overline{w}_{2}\cdots\overline{w}_{n})=1
for every
\alpha\in{\rm supp}^{o}({\bf w})
. Thus

\Delta^{\omega_{\alpha}}(\dot{w}_{1}\dot{w}_{2}\cdots\dot{w}_{n})=\Delta^{\omega_{\alpha}}(t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}),\hskip 14.45377pt\forall\,\alpha\in{\rm supp}^{o}({\bf w}).

Lemma
D.4
is then equivalent to saying for every
(q,t)\in\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{{\bf w}}}_{a}
one has

(t^{\omega_{\alpha}})^{2}=a^{-\omega_{\alpha}}\Delta^{\omega_{\alpha}}(\dot{w}_{1}\dot{w}_{2}\cdots\dot{w}_{n}),\hskip 14.45377pt\forall\;\alpha\in{\rm supp}^{o}({\bf w}).

(D.20)

\diamond

For
a\in T
, define a
level set of
\delta_{\bf w}
in
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{{\bf w}}}_{a}
to be any non-empty level set of the map

\delta_{\bf w}|_{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{{\bf w}}}_{a}}:\;\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{{\bf w}}}_{a}\longrightarrow T/{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}.

By (
D.20
),
\delta_{\bf w}
has at most

2^{|{\rm supp}^{o}({\bf w})|}
level sets
in
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{{\bf w}}}_{a}
, and every connected component of
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{{\bf w}}}_{a}
is contained in one such
level set.
Consider the order
2^{|{\rm supp}^{o}({\bf w})|}
sub-group

T^{(2)}_{{\rm supp}^{o}({\bf w})}=\{\alpha^{\vee}(\pm 1):\alpha\in{\rm supp}^{o}({\bf w})\}

of
T^{(2)}=\{t\in T:t^{2}=e\}
.
Note that for each
a\in T
,
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{{\bf w}}}_{a}
is
T^{(2)}_{{\rm supp}^{o}({\bf w})}
-invariant for the
diagonal
T
-action on
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T
.

Lemma D.6
.

For any
a\in T
, there are precisely
2^{|{\rm supp}^{o}({\bf w})|}
level sets of

\delta_{\bf w}
in
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{{\bf w}}}_{a}
, each pair mutually
isomorphic by the action of a unique element in
T^{(2)}_{{\rm supp}^{o}({\bf w})}
.

Proof.

By the
T
-equivariance of

\delta_{\bf w}:{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T\to T/{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
in (
D.18
),
the group
T^{(2)}_{{\rm supp}^{o}({\bf w})}

acts freely and transitively on the collection of all level sets of
\delta_{\bf w}
in
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{{\bf w}}}_{a}
.

Q.E.D.

Take now the special representative
\overline{{\bf w}}=(\overline{w}_{1},\overline{w}_{2},\ldots,\overline{w}_{n})\in{\bf w}T^{n}
, and let

\Sigma^{\overline{{\bf w}}}=\{(q,t)\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T:\,t^{-2}\tau_{\overline{{\bf w}}}(q)\in T^{w},\,t\in{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}\}\subset\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\overline{{\bf w}}}_{e}.

(D.21)

Pick any
q_{0}\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}
. Then
\tau_{\overline{{\bf w}}}(q_{0})\in{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
by (
D.5
). Pick any
t_{0}\in{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
such that
t_{0}^{2}=\tau_{\overline{{\bf w}}}(q_{0})
. Then
(q_{0},t_{0})\in\Sigma^{\overline{{\bf w}}}
, showing that
\Sigma^{\overline{{\bf w}}}\neq\emptyset
.
Thus
\Sigma^{\overline{{\bf w}}}
is a
level set of
\delta_{\bf w}
in
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\overline{{\bf w}}}_{e}
.

Theorem D.7
.

For any
{\bf w}\in W^{n}
, the sub-variety
\Sigma^{\overline{{\bf w}}}
of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T
is connected.

Theorem
D.7
will be proved in
\lx@sectionsign
D.4
. In the rest of
\lx@sectionsign
D.2
, we use
Theorem
D.7
to describe all the symplectic leaves of
({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T,\pi_{n}\bowtie 0)

for every
{\bf w}\in W^{n}
.

Theorem D.8
.

For any
\dot{{\bf w}}\in{\bf w}T^{n}
and any
a\in T
, the
2^{|{\rm supp}^{o}({\bf w})|}
level sets of
\delta_{\bf w}
in

\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{{\bf w}}}_{a}
are precisely all the connected components of
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{{\bf w}}}_{a}
and are thus also all the
symplectic leaves of
({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T,\pi_{n}\bowtie 0)
contained in
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{{\bf w}}}_{a}
.

Proof.

Consider first when
\dot{{\bf w}}=\overline{{\bf w}}
. The level sets of
\delta_{\bf w}
in

\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\overline{{\bf w}}}_{e}
, being isomorphic to
\Sigma^{\overline{{\bf w}}}\subset\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\overline{{\bf w}}}_{e}
by Lemma
D.6
, are connected
by Theorem
D.7
, and since they are both open and closed, they are all the connected components of
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\overline{{\bf w}}}_{e}
.
For any
a\in T
, choose any
h\in T
such that
h^{-2}=a
. Then
h\cdot\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\overline{{\bf w}}}_{e}=\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\overline{{\bf w}}}_{a}
. By the
T
-equivariance of
\delta_{\bf w}
in (
D.18
), the level sets of
\delta_{\bf w}
in

\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\overline{{\bf w}}}_{a}
are in bijection with the level sets of
\delta_{\bf w}
in
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\overline{{\bf w}}}_{e}
by the
action of
h
and are thus all the connected components of
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\overline{{\bf w}}}_{a}
.
For an arbitrary
\dot{{\bf w}}\in{\bf w}T^{n}
, since
\{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{{\bf w}}}_{a}:a\in T\}=\{\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\overline{{\bf w}}}_{a}:a\in T\}

by Remark
D.2
, the level sets of
\delta_{\bf w}
in

\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{{\bf w}}}_{a}
are also all the connected components of
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{{\bf w}}}_{a}
.

By Proposition
D.3
, the level sets of
\delta_{\bf w}
in

\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{{\bf w}}}_{a}
are precisely all the
symplectic leaves of
({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T,\pi_{n}\bowtie 0)
contained in
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{{\bf w}}}_{a}
.

Q.E.D.

We have the following immediate consequence of Theorem
D.8
.

Corollary D.9
.

Let
{\bf w}\in W^{n}
and
\dot{{\bf w}}\in{\bf w}T^{n}
. The symplectic leaf of
({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T,\pi_{n}\bowtie 0)

through any
(q_{0},t_{0})\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T
consists precisely of all
(q,t)\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T
satisfying

\mu_{\dot{{\bf w}}}(q,t)=\mu_{\dot{{\bf w}}}(q_{0},t_{0})\hskip 14.45377pt\mbox{and}\hskip 14.45377pt\delta_{\bf w}(q,t)=\delta_{\bf w}(q_{0},t_{0}).

In view of Corollary
D.9
, for
{\bf w}\in W^{n}
and
\dot{{\bf w}}\in{\bf w}T^{n}
, it is natural to consider the map

\mu_{\dot{{\bf w}}}\times\delta_{\bf w}:\;{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T\longrightarrow T/T^{w}\times T/{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}},\;(q,t)\longmapsto(t^{-2}\tau_{\dot{{\bf w}}}(q)_{\cdot}T^{w},\,t_{\cdot}{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}).

(D.22)

Let
X^{\dot{{\bf w}}}=(\mu_{\dot{{\bf w}}}\times\delta_{\bf w})({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T)\subset T/T^{w}\times T/{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
be the image.
Symplectic leaves of
({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T,\,\pi_{n}\bowtie 0)
are then
precisely all the level sets of the now surjective map

\mu_{\dot{{\bf w}}}\times\delta_{\bf w}:\;{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T\longrightarrow X^{\dot{{\bf w}}}.

To characterize
X^{\dot{{\bf w}}}
, note that
\mu_{\dot{{\bf w}}}\times\delta_{\bf w}
is
T
-equivariant, where

T
acts on
T/T^{w}\times T/{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
by

h\cdot(a_{\cdot}T^{w},\,a^{\prime}{}_{\cdot}{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}})=(h^{-2}a_{\cdot}T^{w},\,ha^{\prime}{}_{\cdot}{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}),\hskip 14.45377pta,a^{\prime}\in T.

Note that
T
has the same the stabilizer subgroup every point in
T/T^{w}\times T/{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
, which is

{\rm Stab}^{{\bf w}}=\{h\in T:\,h\in{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}},\,h^{2}\in T^{w}\}\subset T.

(D.23)

Let
p_{1}:T/T^{w}\times T/{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}\to T/T^{w}
be the projection to the first factor, and note that
p_{1}
is
T
-equivariant, where
T
acts on

T/T^{w}
by
h\cdot(a_{\cdot}T^{w})=h^{-2}a_{\cdot}T^{w}
for
h,a\in T
.
Recall again that
t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}\in T
is such that

\dot{w}_{1}\dot{w}_{2}\cdots\dot{w}_{n}=\overline{w}_{1}\overline{w}_{2}\cdots\overline{w}_{n}\,t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}
.

Theorem D.10
.

For any
{\bf w}\in W^{n}
and
\dot{{\bf w}}\in{\bf w}T^{n}
, one has

X^{\dot{{\bf w}}}=\{(a_{\cdot}T^{w},a^{\prime}{}_{\cdot}{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}})\in T/T^{w}\times T/{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}:\;a(a^{\prime})^{2}\in t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}\,{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}\}.

(D.24)

Moreover,
X^{\dot{{\bf w}}}
is a single
T
-orbit in
T/T^{w}\times T/{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
and is thus smooth and isomorphic to
T/{\rm Stab^{\bf w}}
.
The subgroup
T^{(2)}_{{\rm supp}^{o}({\bf w})}
of
T
acts freely on
X^{\dot{{\bf w}}}
, and
the restriction of
p_{1}
to
X^{\dot{{\bf w}}}
gives a covering map
p_{1}:X^{\dot{{\bf w}}}\to T/T^{w}

whose fibers are orbits of

T^{(2)}_{{\rm supp}^{o}({\bf w})}
in
X^{\dot{{\bf w}}}
.

Proof.

If
a,a^{\prime}\in T
are such that
(a_{\cdot}T^{w},a^{\prime}{}_{\cdot}{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}})\in X^{\dot{{\bf w}}}
, it follows from (
D.22
) that

a(a^{\prime})^{2}\in t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}\,{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
. Conversely,
suppose that
a,a^{\prime}\in T
are such that
a(a^{\prime})^{2}\in t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}\,{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
. Let
x\in{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
be such that
a(a^{\prime})^{2}=t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}x^{2}
. Let

\sqrt{t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}}
be any element in
T
such that
\sqrt{t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}}^{2}=t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}
. Then

(a_{\cdot}T^{w},a^{\prime}{}_{\cdot}{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}})=h\cdot(e_{\cdot}T^{w},\;\sqrt{t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}}{}_{\cdot}{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}})\in X^{\dot{{\bf w}}},

where
h=a^{\prime}(\sqrt{t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}})^{-1}x^{-1}\in T
. Thus
X^{\dot{{\bf w}}}
is given as in (
D.24
).

As
T
acts transitively on the set of all symplectic leaves of
({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T,\,\pi_{n}\bowtie 0)
, the subset

X^{\dot{{\bf w}}}
of
T/T^{w}\times T/{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
is a single
T
-orbit and is thus also smooth. One checks directly from the definitions
that the stabilizer subgroup of
T
at every point in
X^{\dot{{\bf w}}}
is
{\rm Stab}^{\bf w}
. The map
p_{1}:X^{\dot{{\bf w}}}\to T/T^{w}
, being is
T
-equivariant,
is thus surjective. For any fixed
a_{\cdot}T^{w}\in T/T^{w}
, one has

p_{1}^{-1}(a_{\cdot}T^{w})=\{(a_{\cdot}T^{w},\,a^{\prime}_{\cdot}{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}):\;a^{\prime}\in T,\,(a^{\prime}_{\cdot}{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}})^{2}=a^{-1}t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}{}_{\cdot}{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}\}

which is precisely an orbit of
T^{(2)}_{{\rm supp}^{o}({\bf w})}
in
X^{\dot{{\bf w}}}
.

Q.E.D.

Corollary D.11
.

For any
{\bf w}\in W^{n}
and
\dot{{\bf w}}\in{\bf w}T^{n}
, the map

\mu_{\dot{{\bf w}}}\times\delta_{\bf w}:{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T\to X^{\dot{{\bf w}}}

is a surjective submersion whose level sets are precisely all the symplectic leaves of
({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T,\pi_{n}\bowtie 0)
.

Proof.

As
p_{1}\circ(\mu_{\dot{{\bf w}}}\times\delta_{\bf w}):{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T\to T/T^{w}
is a submersion, and as
p_{1}:X^{\dot{{\bf w}}}\to T/T^{w}
is a covering map,

\mu_{\dot{{\bf w}}}\times\delta_{\bf w}:{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T\to X^{\dot{{\bf w}}}
is also a submersion.

Q.E.D.

For
\dot{{\bf w}}\in{\bf w}T^{n}
, let again
\sqrt{t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}}
be any element in
T
such that

(\sqrt{t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}})^{2}=t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}
, and set

\Sigma^{{\dot{{\bf w}}}}\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm def}}}{{=}}\,\{(q,t)\in{\mathcal{O}}^{w}_{e}\times T:\;\;\,t^{-2}\tau_{{\dot{{\bf w}}}}(q)\in T^{w},\;t\in\sqrt{t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}}\,{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}\},

(D.25)

which is a level set of
\delta_{\bf w}
in
\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\dot{{\bf w}}}_{a}
and thus a symplectic leaf of

({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T,\,\pi_{n}\bowtie 0)
. Note that

\Sigma^{{\dot{{\bf w}}}}=\sqrt{t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}}\cdot\Sigma^{\overline{{\bf w}}}.

(D.26)

We now have the following alternative reformulation of Theorem
D.8
.

Theorem D.12
.

For any
{\bf w}\in W^{n}
and
\dot{{\bf w}}\in{\bf w}T^{n}
, symplectic leaves of
({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T,\,\pi_{n}\bowtie 0)
are precisely all the sub-varieties of

{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T
of the form

h\cdot\Sigma^{{\dot{{\bf w}}}}=\{(q,t)\in{\mathcal{O}}^{w}_{e}\times T:\;\;\,t^{-2}\tau_{{\dot{{\bf w}}}}(q)\in h^{-2}T^{w},\;t\in h\sqrt{t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}}\,{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}\},

where
h\in T
. For
h_{1},h_{2}\in T
,

h_{1}\cdot\Sigma^{{\dot{{\bf w}}}}=h_{2}\cdot\Sigma^{{\dot{{\bf w}}}}
if and only if
h_{1}^{-1}h_{2}\in{\rm Stab}^{{\bf w}}
given in (
D.23
).

Remark D.13
.

Note that for any symplectic leaf
\Sigma

of
({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T,\,\pi_{n}\bowtie 0)
and for any
h\in T
,
h\cdot\Sigma=\Sigma
if and only if
h\in{\rm Stab}^{{\bf w}}
.
For this reason, we call
{\rm Stab}^{{\bf w}}
is the
leaf-stabilizer of
T
in
({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T,\pi_{b}\bowtie 0)
.

\diamond

We have already seen in Proposition
D.3
that every symplectic leaf of
({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T,\pi_{n}\bowtie 0)
has dimension equal to

l({\bf w})+\dim({\rm Im}(1-w))=l({\bf w})+\dim(T^{w})
. We now show that
\Sigma^{{\dot{{\bf w}}}}
is a

2^{|{\rm supp}({\bf w})|}
-to-
1
cover of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T^{w}
.
To this end, let
T
act on
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T
by

h\circ(q,t)=(q,ht),\hskip 14.45377pth\in T,\,(q,t)\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}\times T.

Consider
T^{(2)}_{{\rm supp}({\bf w})}=\{\alpha^{\vee}(\pm 1):\alpha\in{\rm supp}({\bf w})\}=\{h\in{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}:h^{2}=e\}
,
a group of order
2^{|{\rm supp}({\bf w})|}
. It follows from the definitions that
\Sigma^{{\dot{{\bf w}}}}
is invariant under the action of

T^{(2)}_{{\rm supp}({\bf w})}
.

Proposition D.14
.

For any
\dot{{\bf w}}\in{\bf w}T^{n}
, the map

\Sigma^{\dot{{\bf w}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T^{w},\;(q,\,t)\longmapsto(q,\,t^{-2}\tau_{\dot{{\bf w}}}(q)),

is a covering map whose fibers are the orbits of
T^{(2)}_{{\rm supp}({\bf w})}
in
\Sigma^{\dot{{\bf w}}}
.

Proof.

Let
(q,t^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T^{w}
. Writing
t\in\sqrt{t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}}\,{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
as
t=\sqrt{t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}}\,x
for
x\in{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
, then

t^{-2}\tau_{\dot{{\bf w}}}(q)=t^{\prime}
if and only if

x^{2}=(t^{\prime})^{-1}\tau_{\overline{{\bf w}}}(q)
.
By (
D.5
),
\tau_{\overline{{\bf w}}}(q)\in{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
. The equation
x^{2}=(t^{\prime})^{-1}\tau_{\overline{{\bf w}}}(q)
,
regarded as one in
{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
,
then has exactly

2^{|{\rm supp}({\bf w})|}
solutions, consisting of a single
T^{(2)}_{{\rm supp}({\bf w})}
coset in
{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
.

Q.E.D.

Example D.15
.

Consider the special case
when
{\bf w}=(w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})\in W^{n}
is such that
w=w_{1}w_{2}\cdots w_{n}=e\in W
. Then
T^{w}=\{e\}
. Assume also that

{\bf w}=(\dot{w}_{1},\ldots,\dot{w}_{n})\in{\bf w}T^{n}
satisfies
\dot{w}_{1}\dot{w}_{2}\cdots\dot{w}_{n}=e\in G
. Then element

t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}\in T
given by

e=\overline{w}_{1}\cdots\overline{w}_{n}\,t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}
lies in
{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
, and by (
D.19
)
the image of
\tau_{\dot{{\bf w}}}:{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\to T
also lies in
{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
.
Thus the symplectic leaf of

({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T,\pi_{n}\bowtie 0)
through the point
([\dot{w}_{1},\ldots,\dot{w}_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}},e)\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T
is
given by

\Sigma^{\dot{{\bf w}}}=\{(q,t)\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}:\;t^{2}=\tau_{\dot{{\bf w}}}(q)\}.

By Proposition
D.14
, the projection
\Sigma^{\dot{{\bf w}}}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e},(q,t)\mapsto q
, is a
2^{|{\rm supp}({\bf w})|}
-to-
1

covering map.

\diamond

Recall that
\Gamma_{n}=\{[g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}}:g_{1}g_{2}\cdots g_{n}\in B_{-}\}
, with the
T
-action

t\cdot[g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}}=[tg_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}}.

By Proposition
4.1
,
T
-leaves of
(\Gamma_{n},\widetilde{\pi}_{n})
are precisely all the
\Gamma^{\bf w}
’s as
{\bf w}
runs over
W^{n}
, where

\Gamma^{\bf w}=(B{\bf w}B)\cap\Gamma_{n}.

In the remainder of
\lx@sectionsign
D.2
, we determine symplectic leaves in
(\Gamma^{\bf w},\widetilde{\pi}_{n})
for every
{\bf w}\in W^{n}
, which is enough for
the discussion in
\lx@sectionsign
5
on configuration symplectic groupoids. In fact only the cases of

{\bf w}=({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})
for
{\bf u}\in W^{m}
are needed in
\lx@sectionsign
5
. Symplectic leaves in

(G_{m,n},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})
and
(\widetilde{F}_{n},\widetilde{\pi}_{n})
for all integers
m,n\geq 1
are determined in
\lx@sectionsign
D.5
and

\lx@sectionsign
D.6
.

Let
{\bf w}=(w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})\in W^{n}
and choose any
\dot{{\bf w}}=(\dot{w}_{1},\ldots,\dot{w}_{n})\in{\bf w}T^{n}
.
With an arbitrary
\gamma\in\Gamma^{\bf w}
uniquely written as (see (
4.23
))

\gamma=[{c}_{1},\ldots,{c}_{n-1},{c}_{n}b]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}},\hskip 14.45377pt\mbox{where}\;\;(c_{1},\ldots,c_{n})\in C_{\dot{{\bf w}}},\;b\in B,\;c_{1}\cdots c_{n-1}c_{n}b\in B_{-},

(D.27)

and with
b_{-}=c_{1}\cdots c_{n-1}c_{n}b\in B_{-}
, define

\beta_{{\dot{{\bf w}}}}:\;\Gamma^{\bf w}\longrightarrow T/T^{w}\times T/{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}},\;\gamma\longmapsto\left([b]_{0}[b_{-}]_{0}{}_{\cdot}T^{w},\;[b_{-}]_{0}{}_{\cdot}{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}\right).

(D.28)

Let
\sqrt{t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}}\in T
be as in Theorem
D.12
, and let

\Lambda^{\dot{{\bf w}}}=\{\gamma\in\Gamma^{\bf w}:\;[b]_{0}[b_{-}]_{0}\in T^{w},\,[b_{-}]_{0}\in\sqrt{t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}}\,{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}\}=\beta_{{\dot{{\bf w}}}}^{-1}\left(e_{\cdot}T^{w},\,\sqrt{t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}}{}_{\cdot}{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}\right).

(D.29)

Theorem D.16
.

Let
{\bf w}\in W^{n}
and
\dot{{\bf w}}\in{\bf w}T^{n}
.

1) Symplectic leaves of

(\Gamma^{\bf w},\widetilde{\pi}_{n})
are precisely all the non-empty level sets of the map

\beta_{\dot{{\bf w}}}
and all have dimension equal to
l({\bf w})+\dim({\rm Im}(1-w))
.

2) Alternatively, symplectic leaves of
(\Gamma^{\bf w},\widetilde{\pi}_{n})
are all the sub-varieties of
\Gamma^{\bf w}
of the form

h\cdot\Lambda^{\dot{{\bf w}}}=\{\gamma\in\Gamma^{\bf w}:\;[b]_{0}[b_{-}]_{0}\in h^{2}T^{w},\,[b_{-}]_{0}\in h\sqrt{t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}}\,{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}\},

where
h\in T
and
\gamma\in\Gamma^{\bf w}
is written as in (
D.27
).

Proof.

Under the Poisson isomorphism
J_{n}:(\Gamma^{\bf w},\widetilde{\pi}_{n})\to({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T,\pi_{n}\bowtie 0)
in (
3.14
), one
has

J_{n}(\gamma)=([{c}_{1},\ldots,{c}_{n-1},{c}_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}},\;[b_{-}]_{0}),

where
\gamma
is as in (
D.27
). Thus

(\mu_{\dot{{\bf w}}}\times\delta_{\bf w})(J_{n}(\gamma))=({[b]_{0}[b_{-}]_{0})^{-1}}_{\cdot}T^{w},\;[b_{-}]_{0}{}_{\cdot}{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}),

(D.30)

and
J_{n}(\Lambda^{\dot{{\bf w}}})=\Sigma^{\dot{{\bf w}}}
.
Theorem
D.16
now follows from Corollary
D.9
and Theorem
D.12
.

Q.E.D.

Remark D.17
.

By (
D.30
) and Theorem
D.10
,
the image of
\beta_{\dot{{\bf w}}}:\Gamma^{\bf w}\to T/T^{w}\times T/{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
is

Y^{\dot{{\bf w}}}=\{(a_{\cdot}T^{w},a^{\prime}{}_{\cdot}{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}})\in T/T^{w}\times T/{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}:\;a^{-1}(a^{\prime})^{2}\in t_{\dot{{\bf w}}}\,{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}\},

and
symplectic leaves of
(\Gamma^{\bf w},\widetilde{\pi}_{n})
are precisely all the level sets of the surjective submersion

\beta_{{\dot{{\bf w}}}}:\;\Gamma^{\bf w}\longrightarrow Y^{\dot{{\bf w}}}.

Moreover,
\beta_{\dot{{\bf w}}}
is
T
-equivariant, where
T
acts on
T/T^{w}\times T/{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
by

h\cdot(a_{\cdot}T^{w},\;a^{\prime}{}_{\cdot}{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}})=(h^{2}a_{\cdot}T^{w},\;ha^{\prime}{}_{\cdot}{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}),\hskip 14.45377pta,a^{\prime}\in T,

and
Y^{\dot{{\bf w}}}\subset T/T^{w}\times T/{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
is a single
T
-orbit with
{\rm Stab}^{\bf w}
in (
D.23
) as the stabilizer subgroup.

\diamond

Example D.18
.

Consider the special case when
{\bf w}=({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})\in W^{2n}
, where

, so we have the point

[{\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{2n}}\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}\,[\dot{u}_{1},\ldots,\dot{u}_{n},\,\dot{u}_{n}^{-1},\ldots,\dot{u}_{1}^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{2n}}\in\Gamma^{({\bf u},{\bf u}^{-1})}

which is in fact independent of the choice of the representative
\dot{{\bf u}}
for
{\bf u}
.

\dot{{\bf w}}=(\dot{u}_{1},\,\ldots,\,\dot{u}_{n},\,\dot{u}_{n}^{-1},\,\ldots,\,\dot{u}_{1}^{-1})\in{\bf w}T^{2n}.

), i.e. the sub-variety of

,

(c_{1},\ldots,c_{2n-1},c_{2n})\in C_{\dot{{\bf w}}}
and

such that

c_{1}\cdots c_{2n-1}c_{2n}b=b_{-}\in B_{-}\hskip 14.45377pt\mbox{and}\hskip 14.45377pt[b]_{0}[b_{-}]_{0}=e,\;[b_{-}]_{0}\in{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf u}}}.

This example is used in
\lx@sectionsign
5.1
.

\diamond

D.3.
Bott-Samelson coordinates and Lusztig toric charts on
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}

To prepare for the proof of Theorem
D.7
in
\lx@sectionsign
D.4
, we recall in this section some toric charts on
generalized Schubert cells which are of interests of their own (see Remark
D.23
).

Consider again an arbitrary

{\bf w}=(w_{1},w_{2},\ldots,w_{n})\in W^{n}
. For each
i\in[1,n]
, choose a reduced word
{\bf w}_{i}
of
w_{i}
and regard

{\bf w}_{i}
as in
W^{l(w_{i})}
. One then has the concatenation

\widetilde{{\bf w}}=({\bf w}_{1},\,{\bf w}_{2},\,\ldots,\,{\bf w}_{n})=(s_{\alpha_{1}},\;s_{\alpha_{2}},\;\ldots,\;s_{\alpha_{n^{\prime}}})\in W^{n^{\prime}},

(D.31)

where
n^{\prime}=l({\bf w})=l(w_{1})+l(w_{2})+\cdots+l(w_{n})
, and
\alpha_{j}\in\Phi_{0}
for each
j\in[1,n^{\prime}]
.
Using the parametrization of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}
by

C_{\overline{{\bf w}}}=C_{\overline{w}_{1}}\times\cdots\times C_{\overline{w}_{n}}
in (
D.7
) and
the parametrization
g_{{\bf w}_{i}}:{\mathbb{C}}^{l(w_{i})}\to C_{\overline{w}_{i}}
in (
D.4
) for each

i\in[1,n]
, one obtains the isomorphism

q_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}:\;{\mathbb{C}}^{n^{\prime}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w},\;q_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}(z_{1},z_{2},\ldots,z_{n^{\prime}})=[g_{{\bf w}_{1}}(z_{1},\ldots,z_{l_{1}}),\,\ldots,\,g_{{\bf w}_{n}}(z_{l_{n-1}+1},\ldots,z_{n^{\prime}})]_{\widetilde{F}_{n}},

(D.32)

where
l_{i}=l(w_{1})+\cdots l(w_{i})
for
i\in[1,n]
and
n^{\prime}=l_{n}
.
Following
[
5
]
, we call
q_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}:{\mathbb{C}}^{n^{\prime}}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}
the
Bott-Samelson parametrization of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}
defined by
{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}
, and
the resulting coordinates
(z_{1},z_{2},\ldots,z_{n^{\prime}})
on
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}
the

Bott-Samelson coordinates
on
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}
defined by
\widetilde{{\bf w}}
.

We now use
\widetilde{{\bf w}}
to define an open toric chart on
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}
.

Lemma D.19
.

For any
{\bf w}=(w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})\in W^{n}
, the map

\displaystyle\varsigma_{\bf w}:\;(N_{-}\cap Bw_{1}B)\times\cdots\times(N_{-}\cap Bw_{n}B)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w},\;\;(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n})\longmapsto[m_{1},\,\ldots,m_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}}

(D.33)

induces a biregular isomorphism between
(N^{-}\cap Bu_{1}B)\times\cdots\times(N_{-}\cap Bw_{n}B)
and the Zariski open subset

({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w})_{0}
of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}
given by

({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w})_{0}=\{[c_{1},c_{2},\ldots,c_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}}:c_{i}\in C_{\overline{w}_{i}},\,c_{1}\cdots c_{i}\in B^{-}B,\,\forall\,\;i\in[1,n]\}.

Proof.

Let
(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n})\in(N_{-}\cap Bw_{1}B)\times\cdots\times(N_{-}\cap Bw_{n}B)
, and let
c_{i}\in C_{\overline{w}_{i}}
and
b_{i}\in B

for
i\in[1,n]
be defined by

m_{1}=c_{1}b_{1},\;\;\;b_{1}m_{2}=c_{2}b_{2},\;\;\;\ldots,\;\;\;b_{n-1}m_{n}=c_{n}b_{n}.

Then
\varsigma_{\bf w}(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n})=[c_{1},\,c_{2},\,\ldots,\,c_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}}
by definition. As

c_{1}\cdots c_{i}=m_{1}\cdots m_{i}b_{i}^{-1}\in B^{-}B
for each
i\in[1,n]
,
one has
\varsigma_{\bf w}(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n})\in({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w})_{0}
. Furthermore, for any
(c_{1},\ldots,c_{n})\in C_{\dot{{\bf w}}}

such that
[c_{1},\ldots,c_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}}\in({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w})_{0}
,
let
m_{i}\in N_{-}
,
i\in[1,n]
, be given by

m_{1}=[c_{1}]_{-},\hskip 14.45377ptm_{1}m_{2}=[c_{1}c_{2}]_{-},\hskip 14.45377pt\ldots,\hskip 14.45377ptm_{1}m_{2}\cdots m_{n}=[c_{1}c_{2}\cdots c_{n}]_{-}.

Then
(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n})
is the unique element in
(N_{-}\cap Bw_{1}B)\times\cdots\times(N_{-}\cap Bw_{n}B)
such that

\varsigma_{\bf w}(m_{1},m_{2},\ldots,m_{r})=[c_{1},c_{2},\ldots,c_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}}
. Thus
\varsigma_{\bf w}
induces the biregular isomorphism as described.

Q.E.D.

Consider now the concatenation
\widetilde{{\bf w}}
in (
D.31
). Combining
\varsigma_{{\bf w}}
in (
D.33
)
with the open embeddings

m_{{\bf w}_{i}}:({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})^{l(w_{i})}\to N_{-}\cap Bw_{i}B
in (
D.6
) for
i\in[1,n]
, one obtains the open embedding

\sigma_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}:({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})^{n^{\prime}}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}
given by

\sigma_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2},\ldots,\varepsilon_{n^{\prime}})=[m_{{\bf w}_{1}}(\varepsilon_{1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{l_{1}}),\ldots,m_{{\bf w}_{n}}(\varepsilon_{l_{n-1}+1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{n^{\prime}})]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}}.

(D.34)

We call
\sigma_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}:({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})^{n^{\prime}}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}
the
Lusztig toric chart on
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}
defined by
\widetilde{{\bf w}}
.

We now solve the
inverse parameter problem
for the open embedding
\sigma_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}:({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})^{n^{\prime}}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}
.
More specifically, for each
j\in[1,n^{\prime}]
, we will express
\varepsilon_{j}
, regarded as a rational function on
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}
through
(
D.34
), as a monomial of certain regular functions on
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}
. To this end, using
the Bott-Samelson parametrization

q_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}:{\mathbb{C}}^{n^{\prime}}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}
in (
D.32
), define
regular functions
\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}},j}
on
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}
by

\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}},j}(q_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}(z_{1},z_{2},\ldots,z_{n^{\prime}}))=\Delta^{\omega_{\alpha_{j}}}(x_{\alpha_{1}}(z_{1})\overline{s}_{\alpha_{1}}x_{\alpha_{2}}(z_{2})\overline{s}_{\alpha_{2}}\cdots x_{\alpha_{j}}(z_{j})\overline{s}_{\alpha_{j}}),\hskip 14.45377ptj\in[1,n^{\prime}].

(D.35)

Set
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}}\neq 0}=\{q\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}:\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}},1}(q)\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}},2}(q)\cdots\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}},n^{\prime}}(q)\neq 0\}\subset{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}
.

Lemma D.20
.

One has
\sigma_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}(({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})^{n^{\prime}})={\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{\phi_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}\neq 0}
.
Moreover, for
\varepsilon=(\varepsilon_{1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{n^{\prime}})\in({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})^{n^{\prime}}
,

\varepsilon_{j}=\left(\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}},1}^{r_{1,j}}\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}},2}^{r_{2,j}}\cdots\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}},j-1}^{r_{j-1,j}}\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}},j}^{-1}\right)(\sigma_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}(\varepsilon)),\hskip 14.45377ptj\in[1,n^{\prime}],

(D.36)

where for
j\in[1,n^{\prime}]
and
i\in[1,j-1]
,

r_{i,j}=\begin{cases}0,&\hskip 14.45377pt\mbox{if}\;\;\alpha_{i}\in\{\alpha_{i+1},\ldots,\alpha_{j-1}\},\\
-\alpha_{j}(\alpha_{i}^{\vee}),&\hskip 14.45377pt\mbox{if}\;\;\alpha_{i}\notin\{\alpha_{i+1},\ldots,\alpha_{j-1}\},\;\alpha_{i}\neq\alpha_{j},\\
-1,&\hskip 14.45377pt\mbox{if}\;\;\alpha_{i}\notin\{\alpha_{i+1},\ldots,\alpha_{j-1}\},\;\alpha_{i}=\alpha_{j}.\end{cases}

Proof.

For notational simplicity, we set
\phi_{j}=\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}},j}
for
j\in[1,n^{\prime}]
.

Consider the generalized Schubert cell
{\mathcal{O}}^{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}}\subset F_{n^{\prime}}
defined by
{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}\in W^{n^{\prime}}
.
As each
{\bf w}_{i}
, for
i\in[1,n]
, is a reduced word of
w_{i}
, we have the isomorphism

{\bf m}:{\mathcal{O}}^{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}
given by

{\bf m}\left([g_{1},\ldots,g_{n^{\prime}}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n^{\prime}}}\right)=[g_{1}\cdots g_{l_{1}},\,g_{l_{1}+1}\cdots g_{l_{2}},\,\ldots,\,g_{l_{n-1}+1}\cdots g_{n^{\prime}}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}},

where
g_{j}\in Bs_{\alpha_{j}}B
for
j\in[1,n^{\prime}]
.
Let
\varepsilon=(\varepsilon_{1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{n^{\prime}})\in({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})^{n^{\prime}}
and
z=(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n^{\prime}})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{n^{\prime}}

be such that
\sigma_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}(\varepsilon)=q_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}(z)
, i.e.,

[x_{-\alpha_{1}}(\varepsilon_{1}),\,\ldots,\,x_{-\alpha_{n^{\prime}}}(\varepsilon_{n^{\prime}})]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n^{\prime}}}=[x_{\alpha_{1}}(z_{1})\overline{s}_{\alpha_{1}},\,\ldots,\,x_{\alpha_{n^{\prime}}}(z_{n^{\prime}})\overline{s}_{\alpha_{n^{\prime}}}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n^{\prime}}}.

Let
j\in[1,n^{\prime}]
. Then
[x_{-\alpha_{1}}(\varepsilon_{1}),\,\ldots,\,x_{-\alpha_{j}}(\varepsilon_{j})]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{j}}=[x_{\alpha_{1}}(z_{1})\overline{s}_{\alpha_{1}},\,\ldots,\,x_{\alpha_{j}}(z_{j})\overline{s}_{\alpha_{j}}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{j}}
and thus

x_{-\alpha_{1}}(\varepsilon_{1})\cdots x_{-\alpha_{j}}(\varepsilon_{j})t_{j}n_{j}=x_{\alpha_{1}}(z_{1})\overline{s}_{\alpha_{1}}\cdots x_{\alpha_{j}}(z_{j})\overline{s}_{\alpha_{j}}

(D.37)

for some unique
t_{j}\in T
and
n_{j}\in N
. It follows that

\phi_{j}(q_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}(z))=t_{j}^{\omega_{\alpha_{j}}}.

(D.38)

This shows in particular that
\sigma_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}(\varepsilon)\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{\phi_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}\neq 0}
.
Comparing (
D.37
) for
j
and
j-1
, one then has

x_{-\alpha_{j}}(\varepsilon_{j})t_{j}n_{j}=t_{j-1}n_{j-1}x_{\alpha_{j}}(z_{j})\overline{s}_{\alpha_{j}}
.
By (
D.1
), one has

x_{\alpha_{j}}(\varepsilon_{j}^{-1})\overline{s}_{\alpha_{j}}\alpha_{j}^{\vee}(\varepsilon_{j})x_{\alpha_{j}}(\varepsilon_{j}^{-1})t_{j}n_{j}=t_{j-1}n_{j-1}x_{\alpha_{j}}(z_{j})\overline{s}_{\alpha_{j}}\in Bs_{\alpha_{j}}B.

It follows that
\alpha_{j}^{\vee}(\varepsilon_{j})t_{j}=t_{j-1}^{s_{\alpha_{j}}}
, and thus by (
D.38
),

\varepsilon_{j}\phi_{j}(q_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}(z))=(\alpha_{j}^{\vee}(\varepsilon_{j})t_{j})^{\omega_{\alpha_{j}}}=t_{j-1}^{s_{\alpha_{j}}\omega_{\alpha_{j}}}.

Writing
s_{\alpha_{j}}\omega_{\alpha_{j}}=\sum_{\alpha\in\Phi_{0}}(s_{\alpha_{j}}\omega_{\alpha_{j}},\alpha^{\vee})\omega_{\alpha}
, one then has

\varepsilon_{j}\phi_{j}(q_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}(z))=\prod_{\alpha\in\Phi_{0}}(t_{j-1}^{\omega_{\alpha}})^{(s_{\alpha_{j}}\omega_{\alpha_{j}},\,\alpha^{\vee})}=\prod_{\alpha\in\Phi_{0}}(t_{j-1}^{\omega_{\alpha}})^{(\omega_{\alpha_{j}}-\alpha_{j},\alpha^{\vee})}.

If
\alpha\notin\{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{j-1}\}
, then

t_{j-1}^{\omega_{\alpha}}=\Delta^{\omega_{\alpha}}(x_{\alpha_{1}}(z_{1})\overline{s}_{\alpha_{1}}\cdots x_{\alpha_{j-1}}(z_{j-1})\overline{s}_{\alpha_{j-1}})=1
by
(
D.3
). If
\alpha\in\{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{j-1}\}
, then
t_{j-1}^{\omega_{\alpha}}=\phi_{j_{\alpha}}(q_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}(z))
, where

j_{\alpha}={\rm max}\{i\in[1,j-1]:\alpha=\alpha_{i}\}
. With
r_{1,j},\ldots,r_{j-1,j}
as described, one thus has

\varepsilon_{j}=\frac{1}{\phi_{j}(\sigma_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}(\varepsilon))}(\phi_{1}(\sigma_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}(\varepsilon)))^{r_{1,j}}(\phi_{2}(\sigma_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}(\varepsilon)))^{r_{2,j}}\cdots(\phi_{j-1}(\sigma_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}(\varepsilon)))^{r_{j-1,j}}.

Furthermore, given
q=q_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}(z)\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}}\neq 0}
, one sees by induction on
j
that there exist
unique
\varepsilon_{j}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
and
unique
t_{j}\in T
and
n_{j}\in N
for
j\in[1,n^{\prime}]
such that (
D.37
) holds. Thus
\sigma_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}(\varepsilon)=q_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}(z)
.
This shows that
\sigma_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}(({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})^{n})={\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{\phi_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}\neq 0}
.
This finishes the proof of Lemma
D.20
.

Q.E.D.

Remark D.21
.

For
u\in W
let
N_{u}=N\cap\overline{u}N_{-}\overline{u}^{\,-1}
so that
C_{\overline{u}}=N_{u}\overline{u}
.
For
{\bf w}=(w_{1},w_{2},\ldots,w_{n})\in W^{n}
, one then has the isomorphism

N_{w_{1}}\times N_{w_{2}}\times\cdots\times N_{w_{n}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w},\;(g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n})\longmapsto[g_{1}\overline{w}_{1},g_{2}\overline{w}_{2},\ldots,g_{n}\overline{w}_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}}.

(D.39)

Recall also from
[
8
]
that one has
generalized minors

\Delta_{u\omega_{\alpha},v\omega_{\alpha}}(g)=\Delta^{\omega_{\alpha}}(\overline{u}^{\,-1}g\overline{v}),\hskip 14.45377ptg\in G,

where
u,v\in W
and
\alpha\in\Phi_{0}
.
Using the parametrization of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}
in (
D.39
), one can also express the functions
\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}},j}\in{\mathbb{C}}[{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}]
as follows:
for
i\in[1,n]
and
j\in[l_{i-1}+1,l_{i}]
,

\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}},j}([g_{1}\overline{w}_{1},g_{2}\overline{w}_{2},\ldots,g_{n}\overline{w}_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{n}})=\Delta_{\omega_{\alpha_{j}},\,s_{l_{i-1}+1}\cdots s_{j}\omega_{\alpha_{j}}}([g_{1}\overline{w}_{1}\cdots g_{i-1}\overline{w}_{i-1}g_{i}),

where we have set
s_{k}=s_{\alpha_{k}}
for
k\in[1,n^{\prime}]
.
In the special case when
n=1
so
{\bf w}=w\in W
and
{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}=(s_{\alpha_{1}},\cdots,s_{\alpha_{l}})
is a reduced word for
w
,
we can parametrize
{\mathcal{O}}^{w}=BwB/B\subset G/B
by
N_{w}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{w},g\mapsto g\overline{w}_{\cdot}B
,
and (
D.36
) can be rewritten as

\varepsilon_{j}=\frac{\prod_{\alpha\neq\alpha_{j}}\Delta_{\omega_{\alpha},\,s_{\alpha_{1}}\cdots s_{\alpha_{j}}\omega_{\alpha}}(g)^{-\alpha_{j}(\alpha^{\vee})}}{\Delta_{\omega_{\alpha_{j}},\,s_{\alpha_{1}}\cdots s_{\alpha_{j}}\omega_{\alpha_{j}}}(g)\Delta_{\omega_{\alpha_{j}},\,s_{\alpha_{1}}\cdots s_{\alpha_{j-1}}\omega_{\alpha_{j}}}(g)},\hskip 14.45377ptj\in[1,l],\,g\in N_{w}.

(D.40)

In this case, the fact that
\sigma_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}(({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})^{l})={\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{\phi_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}\neq 0}
also follows from
[
23
, Proposition 5.2, Corollary 6.6]
, and
(
D.40
) has been proved in

[
23
, Theorem 7.1]
. Equivalent formulations of (
D.40
)
can be found in
[
2
, Theorem 1.4]
and
[
8
, Theorem 2.19]
.

\diamond

Corollary D.22
.

In the context of Lemma
D.20
, one has the isomorphism

\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}}=(\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}},1},\ldots,\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}},n^{\prime}}):\;\;{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}}\neq 0}\longrightarrow({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})^{n^{\prime}}.

(D.41)

By Lemma
D.20
,
\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}}:{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}}\neq 0}\rightarrow({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})^{n^{\prime}}
is the
inverse of
\sigma_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}:({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})^{n^{\prime}}\to{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{\phi_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}\neq 0}
up to an invertible monomial transformation
on
({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})^{n^{\prime}}
.

Remark D.23
.

The functions
\{\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}},1},\ldots,\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}},n^{\prime}}\}
form an initial cluster for a cluster structure on
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}
defined by
K. Goodearl and M. Yakimov using the theory of symmetric Poisson CGLs
[
12
]
, an aspect of generalized Schubert cells that
will be explored elsewhere.

\diamond

D.4.
Proof of Theorem
D.7

Let again
{\bf w}=(w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})\in W^{n}
and
we return to the (non-empty) sub-variety
\Sigma^{\overline{{\bf w}}}
of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}\times T
given
in (
D.21
), i.e.,

\Sigma^{\overline{{\bf w}}}=\{(q,\,t)\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T:\;t\in{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}},\,t^{-2}\tau_{\bf w}(q)\in T^{w}\}.

We now prove Theorem
D.7
which states that
\Sigma^{\overline{{\bf w}}}
is connected.

Let
A^{\bf w}=\{(q,t,t^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}\times T^{w}:t^{-2}\tau_{\overline{{\bf w}}}(q)=t^{\prime}\}
.
Then
A^{\bf w}\to\Sigma^{\overline{{\bf w}}},(q,t,t^{\prime})\mapsto(q,t)
, is an isomorphism. We thus only need to show that
A^{\bf w}
is connected.
Using the fact that for
a_{1},a_{2}\in T
,
a_{1}=a_{2}
if and only if
a_{1}^{\omega_{\alpha}}=a_{2}^{\omega_{\alpha}}
for all
\alpha\in\Phi_{0}
, one has

A^{\bf w}=\{(q,t,t^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}\times T^{w}:\;(\tau_{\overline{{\bf w}}}(q))^{\omega_{\alpha}}=(t^{2}t^{\prime})^{\omega_{\alpha}},\,\forall\,\alpha\in\Phi_{0}\}.

Since
T^{w}\subset{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}=\{t\in T:t^{\omega_{\alpha}}=1,\,\forall\,\alpha\in{\rm supp}^{o}({\bf w})\}
and

\tau_{\overline{{\bf w}}}({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e})\subset{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}
, one has

A^{\bf w}=\{(q,t,t^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}\times T^{w}:\;(\tau_{\overline{{\bf w}}}(q))^{\omega_{\alpha}}=(t^{2}t^{\prime})^{\omega_{\alpha}},\,\forall\,\alpha\in{\rm supp}({\bf w})\}.

Choose a reduced word for each
w_{i}
and consider again the
sequence
{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}\in W^{n^{\prime}}
of simple reflections in (
D.31
),
where
n^{\prime}=l({\bf w})
, and the sequence
\{\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}},j}:j\in[1,n^{\prime}]\}
of
regular functions on
{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}
given in (
D.35
).
For
\alpha\in{\rm supp}({\bf w})
, let
j_{\bullet}(\alpha)\in[1,n^{\prime}]
be the maximal
j\in[1,n^{\prime}]
such that
\alpha=\alpha_{j}
. By (
D.5
),
one has
(\tau_{\overline{{\bf w}}}(q))^{\omega_{\alpha}}=\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}},j_{\bullet}(\alpha)}(q)
for all
\alpha\in{\rm supp}({\bf w})
and
q\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}
.
On the other hand, since an element
g\in G
lies in
B_{-}B
if and only if
\Delta^{\omega_{\alpha}}(g)\neq 0
for all
\alpha\in\Phi_{0}
,
one has by (
D.5
) again that

{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}=\{q\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}:\;\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}},j_{\bullet}(\alpha)}(q)\neq 0,\,\forall\,\alpha\in{\rm supp}({\bf w})\}\}
.
It follows that

A^{\bf w}=\{(q,t,t^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}\times{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}\times T^{w}:\;\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}},j_{\bullet}(\alpha)}(q)=(t^{2}t^{\prime})^{\omega_{\alpha}},\,\forall\,\alpha\in{\rm supp}({\bf w})\}.

Consider the Zariski open subset
A^{\bf w}_{0}
of
A^{\bf w}
given by

A^{\bf w}_{0}=A^{\bf w}\cap({\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{\phi_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}\neq 0}\times\widetilde{T}^{\bf w}\times T^{w}).

Let
J=\{j_{\bullet}(\alpha):\alpha\in{\rm supp}({\bf w})\}
and
J^{o}=[1,n^{\prime}]\backslash J
. Under the isomorphism

\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}}\times{\rm Id}_{{\widetilde{T}^{{\bf w}}}\times T^{w}}:\;\;{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{\phi_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}\neq 0}\times\widetilde{T}^{\bf w}\times T^{w}\longrightarrow({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})^{n^{\prime}}\times\widetilde{T}^{\bf w}\times T^{w},

the sub-variety
A^{\bf w}_{0}\subset{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{\phi_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}\neq 0}\times\widetilde{T}^{\bf w}\times T^{w}
is then defined by the equations

\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}},j_{\bullet}(\alpha)}(q)=(t^{2}t^{\prime})^{\omega_{\alpha}},\,\hskip 14.45377pt\forall\,\alpha\in{\rm supp}({\bf w})

on
(q,t,t^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{\phi_{\widetilde{{\bf w}}}\neq 0}\times\widetilde{T}^{\bf w}\times T^{w}
.
Thus
A^{\bf w}_{0}\cong({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})^{|J^{o}|}\times\widetilde{T}^{\bf w}\times T^{w}\cong({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})^{d}
, where

d:=n^{\prime}+\dim({\rm Im}(1-w))
.
Now the complement of
A^{\bf w}_{0}
in
A^{\bf w}
is

\bigcup_{j\in J^{o}}Z_{j}
, where

Z_{j}=\{(q,t,t^{\prime})\in A^{\bf w}:\;\phi_{{\widetilde{{\bf w}}},j}(q)=0\}

for
j\in J^{o}
. Thus
\dim Z_{j}<d
for each
j\in J^{o}
. As
A^{\bf w}

is smooth of dimension
d
,
A^{\bf w}_{0}
is dense in
A^{\bf w}
.
Since
A^{\bf w}_{0}\cong({\mathbb{C}}^{\times})^{d}
is connected, it follows that
A^{\bf w}
is connected.

This finishes the proof of Theorem
D.7
.

D.5.
Symplectic leaves in generalized double Bruhat cells

Assume again that
G
is connected and simply connected.
For any integers
m,n\geq 1
, we describe in this section all the symplectic leaves of the
T
-Poisson variety
(G_{m,n},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})
introduced in

\lx@sectionsign
4.3
and
\lx@sectionsign
C.1
. By Corollary
C.4
, the
T
-leaves of
(G_{m,n},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})
are precisely the
generalized double Bruhat cells in
G_{m,n}
. It is thus enough to determine symplectic leaves in all generalized Bruhat cells. We remark that
symplectic leaves in
(G_{1,1},\widetilde{\pi}_{1,1})\cong(G,\pi_{\rm st})
are determined by M. Kogan and A. Zelevinsky in

[
13
]
.

Let thus
{\bf u}=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{m})\in W^{m}
and
{\bf v}=(v_{1},\ldots,v_{n})\in W^{n}
be arbitrary.
By Corollary
C.8
, one has a
T
-equivalent
isomorphism
K_{{\bf u},\dot{{\bf v}}}:(G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})\to({\mathcal{O}}^{({\bf u},{\bf v}^{-1})}_{e}\times T,\pi_{m+n}\bowtie 0)

for each choice of
\dot{{\bf v}}\in{\bf v}T^{n}
. As symplectic leaves of
({\mathcal{O}}^{({\bf u},{\bf v}^{-1})}_{e}\times T,\pi_{m+n}\bowtie 0)

are determined by Theorem
D.12
,
we will use
K_{{\bf u},\dot{{\bf v}}}
and Theorem
D.12
for a special choice of
\dot{{\bf v}}
to determine all the
symplectic leaves of
(G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})
.

For
w\in W
, let
\overline{\overline{w}}=(\overline{w^{-1}})^{\,-1}\in wT
(see
[
8
,
\lx@sectionsign
1.4]
).
For representatives of
{\bf u}
and
{\bf v}
, we choose

\overline{{\bf u}}=(\overline{u}_{1},\ldots,\overline{u}_{m})\in{\bf u}T^{m}\hskip 14.45377pt\mbox{and}\hskip 14.45377pt\overline{\overline{{\bf v}}}=(\overline{\overline{v}}_{1},\,\ldots,\,\overline{\overline{v}}_{n})\in{\bf v}T^{n}.

Recall that

C_{\overline{{{\bf u}}}}=C_{{\overline{u}}_{1}}\times\cdots\times C_{\overline{{u}}_{m}}
and

C_{\overline{\overline{{\bf v}}}}=C_{\overline{\overline{v}}_{1}}\times\cdots\times C_{\overline{\overline{v}}_{n}}
.
Introduce

{\mathcal{G}}^{\overline{{\bf u}},\overline{\overline{{{\bf v}}}}}=\{({c}_{1},\ldots,{c}_{m},b,b_{-},{c}_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,{c}^{\prime}_{n})\in C_{\overline{{\bf u}}}\times B\times B_{-}\times C_{\overline{\overline{{\bf v}}}}:\,{c}_{1}\cdots{c}_{m}b=b_{-}{c}^{\prime}_{1}\cdots{c}^{\prime}_{n}\}.

(D.42)

One can then parametrize
G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}
by
{\mathcal{G}}^{\overline{{\bf u}},\overline{\overline{{{\bf v}}}}}
by sending

({c}_{1},\ldots,{c}_{m},b,b_{-},{c}_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,{c}^{\prime}_{n})\in{\mathcal{G}}^{\overline{{\bf u}},\overline{\overline{{{\bf v}}}}}

to

{\bf g}=\left([{c}_{1},\ldots,{c}_{m-1},{c}_{m}b]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m}},\,[b_{-}{c}^{\prime}_{1},{c}^{\prime}_{2},\ldots,{c}^{\prime}_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{-n}}\right)\in G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}.

(D.43)

Let
u=u_{1}\cdots u_{m}\in W
,
v=v_{1}\cdots v_{n}\in W
, and recall that
T^{uv^{-1}}=\{a(a^{-1})^{uv^{-1}}:a\in T\}
.
Set

T^{u,v}=\{(a^{-1})^{u}a^{v}:a\in T\}=\{a^{v}:a\in T^{uv^{-1}}\}.

Let again
{\rm supp}^{o}({\bf u},{\bf v})=\{\alpha\in\Phi_{0}:u_{i}\omega_{\alpha}=v_{j}\omega_{\alpha}=\omega_{\alpha},\forall\;i\in[1,m],j\in[1,n]\}
, and
set

\widetilde{T}^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}=\{t\in T:t^{\omega_{\alpha}}=1,\;\forall\;\alpha\in{\rm supp}^{o}({\bf u},{\bf v})\}.

With
{\bf g}\in G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}
expressed as in (
D.43
), define now

\chi_{{{\bf u}},{{\bf v}}}:\;\;G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}\longrightarrow(T/T^{u,v})\times(T/\widetilde{T}^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}),\;\;{\bf g}\longmapsto\left([b]_{0}[b_{-}]_{0}^{v}{}_{\cdot}T^{u,v},\;[b]_{0}{}_{\cdot}\widetilde{T}^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}\right).

(D.44)

Recall that
T
acts on
G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}\subset G_{m,n}
by (
4.27
).
Recall also that
l({\bf u})=l(u_{1})+\cdots+l(u_{m})
and
l({\bf v})=l(v_{1})+\cdots+l(v_{n})
.

Theorem D.24
.

For any
{\bf u}\in W^{m}
and
{\bf v}\in W^{n}
,
symplectic leaves of
(G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})
are all the (non-empty) level sets of the map

\chi_{{{\bf u}},{{\bf v}}}
in (
D.44
) and all have dimension equal to

l({\bf u})+l({\bf v})+\dim({\rm Im}(1-uv^{-1}))
.
Alternatively,

S^{{{\bf u}},{{\bf v}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}\{{\bf g}\in G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}\,\mbox{as in \eqref{eq:bf-g-hat-dot}}:\;\;[b]_{0}\in\widetilde{T}^{{\bf u},{\bf v}},\,[b]_{0}[b_{-}]_{0}^{v}\in T^{u,v}\}

is a symplectic leaf of
(G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})
, and
every symplectic leaf of
(G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})
is of the form

a\cdot S^{{{\bf u}},{{\bf v}}}=\{{\bf g}\in G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}\,\mbox{as in \eqref{eq:bf-g-hat-dot}}:\;\;[b]_{0}\in a^{u}\widetilde{T}^{{\bf u},{\bf v}},\,[b]_{0}[b_{-}]_{0}^{v}\in(a^{2})^{u}T^{u,v}\}

for some
a\in T
.
Furthermore, for
a_{1},a_{2}\in T
,
a_{1}\cdot S^{{{\bf u}},{{\bf v}}}=a_{2}\cdot S^{{{\bf u}},{{\bf v}}}

if and only if
a_{1}^{-1}a_{2}\in\widetilde{T}^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}
and

(a_{1}^{-1}a_{2})^{2}\in T^{uv^{-1}}
.

Proof.

For
{\bf g}\in G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}
as in (
D.43
), the
T
-equivariant Poisson isomorphism

K_{{\bf u},\overline{\overline{{\bf v}}}}:(G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})\to({\mathcal{O}}^{({\bf u},{\bf v}^{-1})}_{e}\times T,\pi_{m+n}\bowtie 0)

in (
C.8
) is given by

K_{{\bf u},\overline{\overline{{\bf v}}}}({\bf g})=([c_{1},\ldots,c_{m-1},c_{m}b,\,(c_{n}^{\prime})^{-1},\cdots,(c_{2}^{\prime})^{-1},(c_{1}^{\prime})^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{m+n}},\,[b_{-}]_{0}).

By (
4.21
), for
{\bf g}\in G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}
as in (
D.43
),
there are unique
({c}_{m+1},\ldots,{c}_{m+n})\in C_{\overline{v_{n}^{-1}}}\times\cdots\times C_{\overline{v_{1}^{-1}}}
and
b_{m+1},\ldots,b_{m+n}\in B
such that

b({c}_{n}^{\prime})^{-1}={c}_{m+1}b_{m+1},\;\;b_{m+1}(c_{n-1}^{\prime})^{-1}={c}_{m+2}b_{m+2},\;\ldots,\;\;b_{n+m-1}(c_{1}^{\prime})^{-1}={c}_{m+n}b_{m+n}.

Then

[{c}_{1},\ldots,{c}_{m-1},{c}_{m}b,\,(c_{n}^{\prime})^{-1},\cdots,(c_{2}^{\prime})^{-1},(c_{1}^{\prime})^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{m+n}}=[{c}_{1},\ldots,{c}_{m},\,{c}_{m+1},\ldots,{c}_{m+n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle F}_{m+n}}
. Since

[b(c_{n}^{\prime})^{-1},\cdots,(c_{2}^{\prime})^{-1},(c_{1}^{\prime})^{-1}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}}=[{c}_{m+1},\ldots,{c}_{m+n-1},{c}_{m+n}b_{m+n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}},

one has
{c}_{1}\cdots{c}_{m-1}{c}_{m}{c}_{m+1}\cdots{c}_{m+n-1}{c}_{m+n}=b_{-}b_{m+n}^{-1}
, and

[b_{m+n}]_{0}=[b]_{0}^{v^{-1}}
.
Thus

[{c}_{1}\cdots{c}_{m-1}{c}_{m}{c}_{m+1}\cdots{c}_{m+n-1}{c}_{m+n}]_{0}=[b_{-}b_{m+n}^{-1}]_{0}=[b_{-}]_{0}([b]_{0}^{-1})^{v^{-1}}.

Let
{\bf w}=({\bf u},{\bf v}^{-1})\in W^{m+n}
, so
\overline{{\bf w}}=(\overline{u}_{1},\ldots,\overline{u}_{m},\overline{v_{n}^{-1}},\ldots,\overline{v_{1}^{-1}},)\in{\bf w}T^{m+n}
. Let
\Sigma^{\overline{{\bf w}}}\subset{\mathcal{O}}^{\bf w}_{e}\times T
be as in (
D.21
). It then follows from
definitions that

K_{{\bf u},\overline{\overline{{\bf v}}}}(S^{{\bf u},{\bf v}})=\Sigma^{\overline{{\bf w}}}
.

By Theorem
D.12
,

\Sigma^{\overline{{\bf w}}}
is a symplectic leaf of

({\mathcal{O}}^{({\bf u},{\bf v}^{-1})}_{e}\times T,\pi_{m+n}\bowtie 0)
of dimension equal to
l({\bf u})+l({\bf v})+{\rm dim}({\rm Im}(1-uv^{-1}))
.
It follows that
S^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}
is a symplectic leaf of
(G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}},\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n})
of the same dimension.

2) follows from Theorem
D.12
and the fact that
K_{{\bf u},\overline{\overline{{\bf v}}}}
is a
T
-equivariant Poisson isomorphism.

Q.E.D.

Remark D.25
.

The Poisson structure
\widetilde{\pi}_{m,n}
is also invariant under the
T
-action

([g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m}},[k_{1},\ldots,k_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{-n}})\cdot t=([g_{1},\ldots,g_{m-1},g_{m}t]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{m}},[k_{1},\ldots,k_{n-1},k_{n}t]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{-n}}).

One checks directly that for any
{\bf u}\in W^{m},{\bf v}\in W^{n}
, and
a\in T
, one has

S^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}\cdot a=\{{\bf g}\in G^{{\bf u},{\bf v}}\,\mbox{as in \eqref{eq:bf-g-hat-dot}}:\;\;[b]_{0}\in a\widetilde{T}^{{\bf u},{\bf v}},\,[b]_{0}[b_{-}]_{0}^{v}\in a^{2}T^{u,v}\}=(a^{-1})^{u}\cdot S^{{{\bf u}},{{\bf v}}}.

\diamond

D.6.
Symplectic leaves of
(\widetilde{F}_{n},\widetilde{\pi}_{n})

Consider now the
T
-Poisson variety
(\widetilde{F}_{n},\widetilde{\pi}_{n})
for
n\geq 1
, and recall that from (
C.10
) that

T
-leaves of
(\widetilde{F}_{n},\widetilde{\pi}_{n})

are precisely of the form

\widetilde{F}_{n}^{{\bf u},v}=\{[g_{1},g_{2},\ldots,g_{n}]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}}\in B{\bf u}B:g_{1}g_{2}\cdots g_{n}\in B_{-}vB_{-}\},

where
{\bf u}\in W^{n}
and
v\in W
. The
T
-equivariant Poisson isomorphism

(\widetilde{F}_{n},\widetilde{\pi}_{n})\to(G_{n,1},\pi_{n,1})
in (
C.9
) gives a
T
-equivariant Poisson isomorphism from

(\widetilde{F}_{n}^{{\bf u},v},\widetilde{\pi}_{n})
to
(G^{{\bf u},v},\widetilde{\pi}_{n,1})
. We can thus use Theorem
D.24
to get a description of all
symplectic leaves of
(\widetilde{F}_{n}^{{\bf u},v},\widetilde{\pi}_{n})
.

More precisely, let
{\bf u}=(u_{1},u_{2},\ldots,u_{n})\in W^{n}
and
v\in W
, and let
u=u_{1}u_{2}\cdots u_{n}\in W
. Write an element in

B{\bf u}B
uniquely as
[c_{1},c_{2},\ldots,c_{n}b]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}}
, where
(c_{1},c_{2},\ldots,c_{n})\in C_{\overline{u}_{1}}\times C_{\overline{u}_{2}}\times\cdots\times C_{\overline{u}_{n}}
and
b\in B
, and let
\underline{c}=c_{1}c_{2}\cdots c_{n}
. Let

\Lambda^{{\bf u},v}=\{[c_{1},c_{2},\ldots,c_{n}b]_{{\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{F}}_{n}}\in B{\bf u}B:\underline{c}\,b\in B_{-}vB_{-},\,[b]_{0}\in\widetilde{T}^{{\bf u},v},[b]_{0}[\underline{c}\,b\,\overline{v^{-1}}]_{0}^{v}\in T^{u,v}\}.

Theorem D.26
.

For any
{\bf u}\in W^{n}
and
v\in W
,

1)
\Lambda^{{\bf u},v}
is a symplectic leaf of
(\widetilde{F}_{n}^{{\bf u},v},\widetilde{\pi}_{n})
of dimension

l({\bf u})+l(v)+\dim({\rm Im}(1-uv^{-1}))
;

2) every symplectic leaf of
(\widetilde{F}_{n}^{{\bf u},v},\widetilde{\pi}_{n})
is of the form
a\cdot\Lambda^{{\bf u},v}
for some
a\in T
. Moreover, for
a_{1},a_{2}\in T
,

a_{1}\cdot\Lambda^{{\bf u},v}=a_{2}\cdot\Lambda^{{\bf u},v}
if and only if
a_{1}^{-1}a_{2}\in\widetilde{T}^{{\bf u},v}
and
(a_{1}^{-1}a_{2})^{2}\in T^{uv^{-1}}
.
